# Route TT conjecture loop (2026-10-13)

- **ID:** tt-loop-1013
- **Stage:** 1-in-progress
- **Type:** CONJECTURE
- **Source:** docs/reports/rh-current-frontier-map.md §TT
- **Statement:** Working log of the hgtl7 conjecture loop on route TT (theta tail, heat–Cassini, Wold residual).

## Goal
Unconditional Lean proof of RH via route TT, a mix, or a new route. Each cycle: conjecture → attack → paper proof → Lean → anti-theatre/anti-laziness.

## Frontier facts used (Lean only)
- `riemannHypothesis_iff_woldNatResidualAllStrictHalfPowerRate` (R:XiHeatCassiniWoldResidualMertensInverseAbel): RH ⟺ Wold residual `R_N = Σ_{p≤N}(1−1/p)³log p/p − log N − C` is `O(N^{−t})` for all `t<½`.
- `riemannHypothesis_iff_woldNatResidualSqrtMeanSpectrum_zero`: same with √N-window means.
- `summable_xiHeatCassiniWoldNatNearIncrementCovariance`: absolute near-pair budget for `2t+η<1` (pure size).
- Walls: memory `rh-core-walls-catalogue` (wall 1 = √x-precision prime counting).

The natural choice ν=ω gives the explicit test `I(τ)=A(τ)+B(τ)≥2.378` with A=[0.7071cos τℓ+4.2426τ sin τℓ]/(¼+τ²), B=2Σ_k[(1−e^{−a_kℓ})/a_k−(a_k(1−e^{−a_kℓ}cos τℓ)+τe^{−a_kℓ}sin τℓ)/(a_k²+τ²)], a_k=2k+½. Values: I(0)=2.828, I(2)=2.530, I(3)=2.255, I(4)=2.017, I(5)=1.902, I(6)=1.951, I(8)=2.458. Fails on τ∈(2.6,7.5); since cos(τx) reaches −1 inside |x|<ℓ for τ≥π/ℓ, η alone cannot repair it (needs ∫η≥0.48>0.45). A certificate must use a designed ν on |x|≥ℓ: a magic-function search, numeric by nature. The signed (full PBAP) version is not certified by the natural dual either. |

| — | **Synthesis rows 75–101** | — | — | (1) Corpus-first: the corpus owns the arch−prime Hodge-index law (`archPrimeHankel_negativeIndex_eq_card_add_one`), the parity/pole splits, slice-Taylor/Laguerre positivity, PF₃₇, the kernel strip and edge energies, de Bruijn/Newman/CSV, VK and Ingham–Huxley. (2) Every linear or quadratic positivity around the explicit formula reduces to the index law. (3) Invariant (row 78): any producer must yield ψ(x)=x+O(x^{½+η}); configuration discontinuity is not the obstruction. (4) Unmapped region: a global, nonlinear Euler×FE coupling inside the strip that is not a reformulation of the zero measure. (5) Hamburger/Kaczorowski–Perelli: degree-1 conductor-1 FE solutions form one ray, so there is no deformation family and continuity methods need an external parameter (heat time, aperture), along which slack degenerates exactly at ζ. |

At complex s (in particular s=ρ) the two factors are not conjugate, so there is no sign. Ring identities among Eisenstein series are the Rankin–Selberg unfolding (rows 9, 27): positivity lives on the real Mellin axis, the duality law again. |

In function fields, curves of the same genus share the arch, hence fat cones (row 205). UNIFYING PRINCIPLE: cone fatness = number of objects sharing the archimedean factor. Function fields: many (fat). ζ·L′: arch_{L′} absorbs ζ (fat, row 203). ζ alone: unique by Hamburger (possibly a point, i.e. (N1)). (N1) is "Hamburger uniqueness made positive". |

| S385 | SYNTHESIS of rows 358–384, the NATURE OF THE MISSING STRUCTURE, as forced by the laws L1–L9 and rows 378–384. Conclusion: every route converges on one object, a global, exact, half-twisted polarisation of the weight-1 H¹=O/(Ξ), equivalently a Krein string for the Weil accelerant. Known geometry supplies it only in characteristic p (Castelnuovo on C×C), and PBAP degenerates there: blindness at every degree kills all tests, so in function fields positivity comes from FAT cones (many curves), never from PBAP. | 415 | ANALYTIC NULL-FACE RIGIDITY OF PBAP (extends row 414 from arc measures to every Kelvin-symmetric charge configuration). (b) RUNGE SEPARATION: on the cylinder ℝ/ℤ×(0,∞), traces of such Φ (and of E²(E+1)²Φ, inverting the radial ODE locally) are dense on any compact K whose complement keeps the axis connected to every component. The only obstruction is a component of the complement that reaches the cusp but not the axis, i.e. K must SEPARATE cusp from axis. (c) Kelvin maps the upper region U (above the scallops) into the unit disc (the lower region, which reaches the axis at the cusp 0 of S(F)), so a Kelvin pair with a part in U has its image part in a FREE region, which forces that part, and hence ν, to vanish. The only Kelvin-fixed separating support is the unit circle. (d) UNCONDITIONALLY no compactly supported test is null: Ĝ of exponential type L has O(LT) zeros up to T, while Ξ² has ~(T/π)log T (Riemann–von Mangoldt), so Ξ²∤Ĝ unless Ĝ≡0 | THEOREM (paper; same technical lemma as row 414) | rows 386, 392c, 405, 411a, 414; corpus `TwistedArcN1Reduction`, `TwistedArcMinimumPrincipleProof`, `ModularArcWhittakerPositivity`, `DoublyBlindCorePoleDistance` | Meaning: within the analytic witness class, the unconditional null face of the blind PD cone is EXACTLY ONE RAY G₀. Remaining non-analytic witnesses are the compact Ψ-blind cores (the other lane's rigidity results suggest there are none at small support ratios). | 416 | POLE-WEIGHT INTERVAL THEOREM (unifies rows 391, 402, 403; partially closes the κ>d case). On blind tests the prime sum vanishes for EVERY weight system, so W_{β,λ}(g)=β·pole(g)+arch(g)=A(g)+(β−1)pole(g), which depends on neither λ nor ζ. (d) c*>0 heuristic: a minimizing sequence for A/\|pole\| cannot shed negative pole to \|x\|→∞. Far pole needs low-frequency far mass; blindness at the dense nodes log n then forces a compensating chirp (frequency ~e^x) whose PD padding costs ~P·L in arch for pole gain P (stationary phase: amplitude ~e^{−L/2} over bandwidth ~e^L). | 443 | BEURLING–DENY REDUCTION OF THE WEIL FORM. With W=M+P (row 442), M Markovian, normal contraction gives M(\|f\|) ≤ M(f), so W(f) ≥ W(\|f\|) − Δ(f) with Δ(f)=P(\|f\|)−P(f) ≥0 (as \|⟨e^{±u/2},f⟩\| ≤ ⟨e^{±u/2},\|f\|⟩). Using the corpus divisor-moment identity ∫\|ζ(½+it)𝓜f(½+it)\|²n^{−½−it}dt=2πΣ_m d(m)Ψ_f(mn), a core f (supported in [α,β], ∫f=0) is blind ⟺ F:=Σ_{k≥1}f(k·) (Mellin ζ(s)𝓜f(s), the Nyman–Beurling sum; F=O(x^N) at 0 by Euler–Maclaurin since ∫f=0) satisfies ∫_0^∞F(x)F(nx)dx=0 for every n ≥2. | 450 | PITT/BECKNER AS A SOURCE OF THE ARCHIMEDEAN POSITIVITY. Beckner's sharp Pitt inequality ∫\|ξ\|^{−α}\|f̂\|² ≤ C_α∫\|x\|^α\|f\|², with C_α=π^α[Γ((1−α)/4)/Γ((1+α)/4)]², differentiated at α=0 gives ∫log\|x\|\|f\|²+∫log\|ξ\|\|f̂\|² ≥ (ψ(¼)−log π)‖f‖² = Ω(0)‖f‖². The two log-weights become ±i∂_τ, and their sum is ∫\|φ̂\|²Ω/2π. So Beckner's log-uncertainty in log coordinates is literally the pointwise statement Ω(τ) ≥ Ω(0): trivial, with the anomaly as its sharp constant. The Weil form is the σ-derivative at ½ of ∫\|φ̂\|²log\|ξ(σ+iτ)\|²dτ. "Pitt monotonicity" of ξ (nondecreasing in σ ≥½ for every φ) ⟺ RH, while for ζ's archimedean factor alone it is Beckner. LAW: Pitt/Beckner is exactly the archimedean half of Weil positivity, and the arithmetic half is the same monotonicity for the Poisson-summation multiplier ζ, which is RH itself. |

| 452 | CORES AS EXACT "HAAR MOLLIFIERS". On the critical line a core's null spectrum is \|ζ(½+it)𝓜f(½+it)\|², and blindness says this weight is orthogonal to n^{−it} for every n ≥2 (Bohr–Haar-like: it sees only the constant Dirichlet mode). The ideal mollifier 𝓜=1/ζ gives \|ζ𝓜\|²≡1, exactly Haar but not compactly supported. Nyman–Beurling/Báez-Duarte mollifiers with ζ𝓜_N→1 in L²(dt/\|s\|²) exist iff RH. Under ¬RH the NB distance is bounded below by the value at the off-line zero (H² point evaluation), but approximate blindness is weaker than ζ𝓜≈1 and survives. The NB/Báez-Duarte machinery (mollifier optimisation, twisted second moments ∫\|ζ\|²(h/k)^{it}) is the natural toolkit for constructing them, e.g. an implicit-function correction from an NB mollifier at large support ratio. | 457 | ANATOMY OF THE NULL EDGE AT INFINITY (row 456) VIA END PROFILES. | 465 | RIEMANN'S Φ IS A POSITIVE GROUND STATE OF THE WEIL OPERATOR (Beurling–Deny form of the Weil functional). The ground-state identity ⟨Φv,KΦv⟩ = −½∫∫k(x−y)Φ(x)Φ(y)(v(x)−v(y))² then gives, for every real test f=Φv: W_Λ(f) = ½E_ω(v) + E_Λ(v) − E_cosh(v), with E_ω=∫∫ω(\|x−y\|)ΦΦ(Δv)², E_Λ=Σ_nΛ(n)n^{−½}∫Φ(y)Φ(y+log n)(v(y+log n)−v(y))²dy, E_cosh=∫∫cosh((x−y)/2)ΦΦ(Δv)², all ≥0. Routing a cosh jump of length d through prime and ω jumps costs a log log N factor (row 335), and the cumulative intensities cosh vs Σ_{n≤e^X}Λ(n)n^{−½} differ by the PNT error, so the domination IS the √x-precision balance (wall 1). Since v=f/Φ blows up like exp(πe^{2\|x\|}), the Φ⊗Φ weights do not localize: tests probing high zeros live where 1/Φ is astronomically large. Kept: the cleanest exact identity of the session. Φ makes the Weil operator a Markov-type Dirichlet difference with an explicit positive ground state, and RH becomes a Dirichlet-form comparison between "primes + archimedean" and "pole". |

## Row 481 — step back (user: Gordian knot / reframe). Diagnosis and the one missing object
- Diagnosis. The obstruction is the target, not the method. M = arch − primes = W − P, and primes = P + (zero oscillation), so "pole/prime cancellation at √x precision" is the zero sum itself. All frames on ℝ-tests (W, M_o, M|_{C=0}, PBAP, Doob/Poincaré, N_κ/Pontryagin) are isometric restatements; the exactness law (threshold resonance, Λ_dB = 0) forbids slack. Verdict on "reformulate again": INSIGNIFICANT.
- The one non-tautological structure is exact and complete: −M is a Hodge-index form. P = hyperbolic plane of the two poles s = 0, 1 (Lean `CompletedZetaPolePlane.polePlaneForm_exact_inertia`); Φ is the ample class (W-null, M(Φ) = −½, M(·,Φ) = −C); nonneg tests are the effective cone (F ≥ 0 ⟹ C(F) > 0: effective meets ample positively); {C = 0} is Φ^{⊥_M} (Lean `WeilMarkovPrimitiveHyperplane`). Exact Hodge decomposition: M(F) = M(F − 2C(F)Φ) − 2C(F)².
- Weil's proof of Hodge index on C×C uses only RR + Serre duality + ampleness (Hartshorne V.1.9); zeros never appear. Hence the unique missing axiom is a dimension function h⁰ on tests with: (i) h⁰ ≥ 0; (ii) h⁰(F) > 0 ⟹ C(F) > 0; (iii) duality h² = h⁰(K − ·); (iv) RR: h⁰ + h² ≥ −½M(F) + ℓ(F) + c. Given (i)–(iv) Hartshorne's argument is verbatim and yields RH. Row 470's "RR ⟺ RH" is this: existence of such h⁰ is RH; the content moved from positivity to a COUNT.
- Deformation routes (Hilbert's proof of Alexandrov–Fenchel, Kähler-cone continuity) are closed by corpus rigidity: arch degree (`WeilConeDegreeHerglotzClassification`), pole weight (`WeilPoleWeightWindow`), cone ⊆ {Λ}. ζ is an isolated critical point; no family with controlled kernel exists.
- Pontryagin N₁ reading: −(log Λ)'(½+iz) ∈ N_{1+2k}; κ computable on Im z > ½ where Q is an absolutely convergent Dirichlet series + digamma. Prior lane refuted the pole-plane index read (`polePlane_index_blind`): the pole plane is zero-blind, so κ cannot be read from P alone. REDISCOVERED.
- Programme for rows 482+: construct h⁰ as a lattice/theta count on PAIRS (m, n) with the divisibility pairing (the prime term is Σ_{m,n} Λ(n) n^{−½} m^{−1} F(log m)F(log mn) up to smoothing), check (ii) and (iii) on the known sectors (Φ, radical E₁ where M = 2S²), and test (iv) against `exists_markovEnergy_neg` (Φ_L has M < 0, so h⁰(nΦ_L) must grow quadratically). Second candidate: Asano/Lee–Yang contraction identities for Ξ = Φ̂ (corpus `lee-yang-asano-lean`), the only other exact-signature mechanism that never estimates.

## Row 482 — C482: Hodge index via Riemann–Roch axioms (wall 5). Verdict: BRIDGE-TAUTOLOGY; law L482
- C482: RH ⟸ ∃ h⁰ ≥ 0 on tests, K, ℓ, c with (a) h⁰(F) > 0 ⟹ F ≡ G ≥ 0 mod rad(M), (b) h⁰(F) + h⁰(K−F) ≥ −½M(F) + ℓ(F) + c. Hartshorne V.1.9 transfers verbatim; the second ample class D + nΦ works for every D because K_M⋆D = O(cosh(y/2)) (Chebyshev only). With K = 0 the second case is symmetric and no second ample class is needed.
- Kill: the axioms are RH outright. Under RH set h⁰(F) := max(−½M(F),0)·1_{C(F)>0}; conversely (a)+(b) on {C=0} give h⁰(±mF) = 0, so −½m²M(F) + mℓ(F) + c ≤ 0 for all m, i.e. M(F) ≥ 0 by homogeneity. In the analytic setting mD is a scalar multiple, so RR degenerates to homogeneity; the geometric content of RR (an independently defined integer h⁰(mD) that grows like ½m²D²) has no analogue. The exact "RR identity" we have is the Hodge decomposition −½M(F) = C(F)² − ½M(F₁), F₁ = F − 2C(F)Φ, with h⁰ = C² (vanishes on the hyperplane) and h¹ = ½M(F₁); h¹ ≥ 0 is RH.
- Doob frame check: −½M(Φv) = ½Var_m(v) − ½𝓔(v) + pole squares; the only component vanishing on {C=0} ⟺ {m(v)=0} is m(v)² itself. Every exact identity we own has Poincaré shape (energy minus variance); none has a vanishing component beyond the pole square.
- L482 (what is missing, final form). A Hodge-index proof needs an independently defined, manifestly nonnegative expression for M restricted to {C = 0} (an "h¹ count" or exact Gram), identified with arch − primes by a proven identity. RR, Pontryagin N₁, Poincaré, Lee–Yang and deformation all reduce to this one object; deformation is closed by rigidity, RR by homogeneity. The exact Gram must be exact (exactness law), so the object is a spectral realization of M|_{C=0} without zeros. Next: the one unconditional exact identity with zero term identically zero is W(Φ⋆v) = 0 (Φ-smoothed PNT at √x precision, row 465 [P]); check Lean status, then use it as the only available exact arithmetic input for an independent expression of M|_{C=0}.

## Row 483 — C483: exact Φ-smoothed PNT and Gram rigidity. Verdicts: REDISCOVERED / SURVIVES (law)

- Gram rigidity law L483. Φ⋆(tests) ⊂ rad(W) unconditionally (bilinear explicit formula). Any exact Gram representation M|_{even, C=0}(F) = Σ_j |⟨F, ψ_j⟩|² (or an integral over a measure) forces ⟨Φ⋆v, ψ_j⟩ = 0 for all even v with C(Φ⋆v) = 0, hence Ξ·ψ̂_j = 0: every Gram vector has Fourier support in the real zeros of Ξ. There is no zero-free exact Gram; densities (continuous spectra) can never appear (explains law 4). A Hilbert–Pólya realization must therefore be an operator whose spectral measure is proven to be the zero comb, never a Gram with named vectors.
- Metzler structure: for disjointly supported F, G ≥ 0, I(F,G) = −M(F,G) = primes(F,G) − arch(F,G) ≥ 0 (prime part ≥ 0; the archimedean kernel off the diagonal is −e^{−|x|/2}/(1−e^{−2|x|}) < 0). So −M is a Metzler kernel in a bump basis; Φ > 0 is its Perron vector (ample class). Perron–Frobenius gives the simple top eigenvalue, never the absence of a second positive one (row 477 did the odd half-line). INSIGNIFICANT.

## Row 489 — C489: Lefschetz/Hodge–Riemann package and the dilation spectral calculus. Verdict: INSIGNIFICANT

Unconditionally, as functions off the zeros, Re ζ'/ζ(½+iτ) = −½Ω(τ) by the functional equation (1 − s = s̄ on the line), so the function part of Ω − p vanishes identically and the whole Weil symbol is the boundary comb 2πΣ_{on-line}δ_γ plus the pole/off-line residues picked up when shifting the Dirichlet-series contour. The pole is the only non-spectral (rank-2) term; the one negative square is exactly its non-spectrality. Nothing beyond the explicit formula. Reflection positivity (Hankel part ≥ 0) fails near the origin because of the archimedean singularity; not an RH criterion.

## Row 491 — C491: pointwise jump-kernel domination in the Doob frame. Verdict: FALSE; law L491
- In the Φ ground-state frame the Poincaré inequality 𝓔 ≥ Var_m (constant exactly 1) would follow from pointwise domination J(u,u′) ≥ m(u)m(u′)/m(ℝ) of the complete-graph kernel. With J_arch = Φ(u)Φ(u′)a(|u−u′|), a(d) = e^{−d/2}/(1−e^{−2d}), and m⊗m/m(ℝ) = 2Φ(u)Φ(u′)cosh(u/2)cosh(u′/2): a(d) → ∞ at d → 0 (domination holds near the diagonal) but a(d) < 2 once d exceeds a threshold d* < log 2, while the first prime jump sits at log 2. In the window d* ≤ |u−u′| < log 2 nothing dominates; above log 2 only the atoms do. L491: no local (kernel-domination, path-counting, Cheeger-by-indicators) argument can give the exact constant; the compensation is global, confirming the uniform sign law in the Doob frame. Indicator tests Φ1_{>c} give an isoperimetric inequality that is RH-insensitive (incomplete Ξ-integrals are dominated by low zeros) and hence a receiver only.

Hence ind₋M = ind₋W + 1 = 2k + 1 exactly, in agreement with the parity split (k+1, k) of row 466. V₀ = (∂² − ¼)(tests) (row 484).

Under RH this is the self-adjoint Hilbert–Pólya operator on the Weil Hilbert space (L²(μ_Z), translations = multiplication by e^{iγa}); the operator is explicit, the Hilbert space is RH. ## Row 494 — C494: Mertens-type positivity transported to the line. Verdict: FALSE; law L494
- At level σ > 1 the only sum-of-squares positivity in classical theory is Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−σ}h(t log n) ≥ 0 for h ≥ 0 (de la Vallée Poussin with h = 2(1 + cos θ)²): it uses Λ ≥ 0 and pointwise h ≥ 0, i.e. positivity of the prime MEASURE against a nonnegative function, not positivity of a PD form. The explicit formula then bounds the zero sum by pole + arch (Landau), giving β < 1 − c/log t.
- On the line the same sum diverges and the regularization subtracts the pole main term; after subtraction the termwise sign is gone, and what remains is W = arch + pole − primes, whose sign is the zero sum. On V₀ the prime main term is −∫_0^∞ g(u)e^{−u/2}du (from ∫g cosh(u/2) = 0), so W(F) = arch(g) + 2∫_0^∞g e^{−u/2}du − 2∫_0^∞ g(u)e^{−u/2}d(ψ(e^u) − e^u): the whole content is the PNT-remainder pairing. L494: measure-positivity of Λ is consumed by the main-term subtraction; only PD-type positivity survives on the line, and that is RH. The ZFR mechanism cannot be tilted to σ = ½.

## Row 495 — C495: de Bruijn–Newman gradient flow with a self-duality pin. Verdict: CIRCULAR
- The forward heat flow on Ξ is the gradient flow of the Calogero energy U = −Σ_{j<k}log|x_j − x_k| of the zeros (dU/dt = −Σẋ_j² ≤ 0); backward collisions are U → ∞. RH ⟺ Λ_dB ≤ 0 (Rodgers–Tao give ≥ 0) ⟺ no collision on (0, ∞) going backward from the asymptotically equispaced configuration. The functional equation (Kramers–Wannier-type self-duality) pins the critical LINE, not the real-rootedness threshold; e^{tu²} tilts preserve evenness and nothing else. What would suffice is a gap lower bound along the backward flow uniform down to t = 0, which is Λ ≤ 0 itself. A regularized U(0) is finite for complex simple zeros too, so finiteness of U is not a criterion. CIRCULAR.

## Row 498 — C498: the pole-free symbol. Verdict: INSIGNIFICANT

## Row 499 — C499: Lee–Yang factorization through the Euler product / BPY sum. Verdict: FALSE (wall 6), REDISCOVERED (Turán)
- LY (real-rooted Fourier transform) is closed under convolution, so RH would follow if Φ were the law of log Z for Z an infinite product of independent positive variables whose logs have LY laws. For σ > 1 the Euler product is exactly such a product: ζ(σ+it)/ζ(σ) = E[X_σ^{−it}], log X_σ = Σ_p N_p log p (N_p geometric), and Γ(s/2)π^{−s/2} is the Mellin transform of |Gaussian|. But the Euler factors are meromorphic, not entire (poles at 2πik/log p), the law of X_σ exists only for σ > 1 (the pole), and no factorization of ξ into entire real-rooted prime factors exists. This is wall 6 (positivity only where the Euler product converges absolutely).
- BPY realizes ξ as the Mellin transform of a positive law via the SUM Y² = Σ_n E_n/(πn)² (independent exponentials), whose Laplace transform √(2λ)/sinh√(2λ) is real-rooted (Pólya frequency). Mellin transforms of partial sums Y_N² are Dirichlet polynomials Γ(s+1)Σ_{n≤N}c_n^{(N)}(πn)^{2(s+1)} with partial-fraction coefficients c_n^{(N)} → (−1)^{n−1}·2 (recovering η(−2s)); two exponentials give zeros on a vertical line, three or more do not (Turán-type off-line zeros of partial sums). Laplace-real-rootedness (PF) and Mellin-real-rootedness (RH) are different properties; the sum structure does not factorize Mellin transforms. REDISCOVERED.

## Row 500 — synthesis after 500 rows: the nature of the problem as the loop sees it
1. Exact structure (all [L] or [P] with Lean capstones): tests = V₀ ⊕ span{Φ, Φ′}; M = W|_{V₀} ⊕ hyperbolic plane; RH ⟺ W ≥ 0 on V₀ = (∂² − ¼)(tests) ⟺ M ≥ 0 on odd tests ⟺ M ≥ 0 on {C = 0} ⟺ M ≥ 0 on either single pole hyperplane; ind₋M = ind₋W + 1 = 2k + 1. The one negative square is the pole and nothing else.
2. Exactness: RH is a threshold statement (Λ_dB = 0; inf{h : Φcosh(hx) Lee–Yang} = Θ − ½; gap of the Doob chain exactly 1 with infinite multiplicity). No inequality with slack, no local comparison (L491), no Krein window data (L488), no measure-positivity of Λ (L494) can decide it.
3. Rigidity: every exact Gram of W on V₀ has vectors spectrally supported on the zeros (L483); Riemann–Roch degenerates to homogeneity (L482); deformations inside explicit-formula families are frozen (rows 414–425, 479); the only free deformation (cosh/heat universal factors) has its threshold equal to the unknown zero-free strip.
4. Consequently the only admissible proof shape is a spectral realization: an a priori self-adjoint (or unitary) object with an a posteriori proven identity between its spectral measure of a vector and the zero comb Σ_γ δ_γ (wall 12). Candidates must carry multiplicative structure (wall 11: log 4 matters, log 6 does not) and must not pass through absolutely convergent Euler products (wall 6).
5. Exact identities in hand that any such construction must reproduce: K_M ⋆ Φ = −cosh(x/2) (Φ-smoothed PNT, row 483); Σ_p log p(P_p − 1) = Ω off the zeros (row 490); the Doob identity M(Φv) = 𝓔(v) − ‖v‖²_m with m Lee–Yang (rows 467, 497); the hyperbolic-plane splitting (row 493).
Next: rows 501+ attack item 4 directly — construct candidate self-adjoint objects from the multiplicative structure (Hecke resolvents R_p, the Doob chain, the reset-plus-Gram decomposition) and test the identity with the exact inputs of item 5, killing by L483 when a candidate's spectral vectors are not forced onto the zeros.

## Row 501 — C501: Hermite's Bezoutian as the spectral realization. Verdict: CIRCULAR (exact), REDISCOVERED
- For the de Branges function E = Ξ − iΞ′ the reproducing kernel is the Bezoutian K(w,z) = [Ξ(z)Ξ′(w̄) − Ξ′(z)Ξ(w̄)]/(π(w̄ − z)), defined without zeros; Hermite's identity gives K = Σ_γ q_γ(z)·q_γ(w̄)·(sign) with q_γ = Ξ/(z − γ), a Gram over the zeros, PD iff all zeros real and simple (L483 realized: the Gram vectors are the kernels at the zeros). Entries of the infinite Bezoutian matrix in the Taylor basis at the center are finite polynomial expressions in the moments ∫x^{2k}Φ; RH ⟺ that matrix is PSD. Taylor coefficients of Ξ′/Ξ at 0 are −2Z(2k+2), Z(s) = Σ_γ γ^{−s} the secondary zeta function; RH ⟺ (Z(2k+2))_k is a Stieltjes moment sequence (off-line pairs make the Hankel form indefinite only in directions resolving scale 1/γ², the detection law in moment form; corpus Hankel criteria). Hermite's theorem for polynomials is algebraic in the roots; for Ξ the algebra is in the zeros again. CIRCULAR.

## Row 502 — C502: Connes' absorption picture as an a priori self-adjoint realization. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (wall 12)
- The scaling action on the adele class space is unitary a priori; the Weil explicit formula is its trace formula with cutoff, and the zero comb enters the trace with a MINUS sign against the Weyl term (log Λ)·2|f̂(0)|² ("absorption lines"). Positivity of a trace of a positive operator against |f̂|² gives only the upper bound Σ_ρ|f̂(ρ)|² ≤ Weyl + pole (the Riemann–von Mangoldt law), never the lower bound 0 ≤ Σ_ρ. The zeros as point spectrum of the cokernel need the Hilbert structure of the quotient, which is Weil positivity. Modular Maass–Selberg densities −Re φ′/φ(½+ir) = Σ_ρ K_ρ(r) are positive for every zero configuration (RH-blind, ULF ledger II). No a priori self-adjoint object with the zero comb as spectral measure of a canonical vector is available; compact quotients of ℝ give arithmetic progressions, Selberg surfaces give Selberg zeros.

## Row 503 — C503: the odd sector as half-line Markov form plus trivial-zero squares plus product pairing. Verdict: SURVIVES (exact law L503, [P])
- For real f supported in [ε, L] (ε > 0) and F = f − f̃ odd: M(F) = 2M(f) − 2M(f, f̃), and weilTest(f, f̃) = f⋆f (convolution, supported in (2ε, 2L)). With the Lévy–Khintchine form Ω(r) = Ω(0) + 2∫_0^∞ a(x)(1 − cos rx)dx (corpus `gammaHardyPhaseRate_sub_zero_eq_integral`), arch(f⋆f) = −∫_0^∞ a(u)(f⋆f)(u)du = −∫∫f(x)f(y)a(x+y), and a(u) = Σ_{k≥0}e^{−(2k+½)u} gives ∫∫f f a(x+y) = Σ_{k≥0}(∫f(x)e^{−(2k+½)x}dx)² = Σ_{k≥0} f̃(−2k)²: the squares of the Mellin transform at the trivial zeros s = 0, −2, −4, …. Hence
  M(f − f̃) = 2[ M(f) + Σ_{k≥0} f̃(−2k)² + Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(f⋆f)(log n) ].
  The reflected archimedean kernel of the odd sector is a manifest sum of squares over the trivial zeros; the product-prime pairing Π(f) = Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(f⋆f)(log n) is ≥ 0 for f ≥ 0. With the odd capstone: RH ⟺ M(f) + Σ_k f̃(−2k)² + Π(f) ≥ 0 for all such f. In the even sector both extra terms enter with a minus sign, M(f + f̃) = 2[M(f) − Σ_k f̃(−2k)² − Π(f)], which carries the one negative square.
- Atomic (Dirichlet-polynomial, narrow-bump) tests a = (a_m)_{m≥2}: Π = Σ_p log p·⟨a, w_p⟩², w_p = (R_p − 1)e_1 = Σ_{j≥1}p^{−j/2}e_{p^j} (products n = p^i p^j only); trivial-zero squares Σ_k⟨a, v_k⟩², v_k = (m^{−2k−½})_m; ratio form 2Σ_p log p·Re⟨a,(R_p − 1)a⟩ with the Hecke resolvent R_p = (1 − p^{−½}S_p)^{−1} on ℓ²(ℕ). Per prime the bracket Q_p(a) = ⟨a,w_p⟩² − 2Re⟨a,(R_p−1)a⟩ = A(x)² + ‖a‖² − ‖a‖²_{P_x} (x = p^{−½}, A the local generating function, P_x the Poisson kernel) is indefinite: Q_p(e_1 + e_2) = x[x(1+x)² − 2] < 0 for p ≥ 3. So there is no per-prime completion of squares; positivity needs the Toeplitz archimedean term and the trivial-zero squares globally (uniform sign law). Lean target if a follow-up needs it: the identity above (Fubini + Lévy–Khintchine + geometric series); deferred.

## Row 504 — [L] L503 formalized: `RHTower/WeilMarkovOddSectorTrivialZeroSquares` (wired after `WeilMarkovOnePoleHyperplane`, standard axioms)
- `markovEnergy_oddExtension_eq`: for real C² f with compact support in [ε, ∞), ε > 0: M(f − f̃) = 2M(f) + 2Σ_{k≥0}(∫f e^{−(2k+½)x})² + 2Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(f⋆f)(log n). Definitions `convSquare`, `trivialZeroMoment`, `productPrimePairing`, `archForm`, `levyEnergy`, `primeSum`.
- Mechanism: polarization of the corpus Lévy form (`integral_archSymbol_mul_normSq_fourier_eq`, `integral_paperFT_sq_mul_gammaBracket`) between the even and odd extensions; the Lévy difference is −4∫_0^∞ω(x)(f⋆f)(x)dx by the support argument (`levy_polarization_pointwise`); ω = Σ_k e^{−(2k+½)x} (`archLevyDensity_eq_tsum`) and Fubini give Σ_k f̃(−2k)² (`integral_archLevyDensity_mul_convSquare_eq_tsum`); the prime part by `crossCorr_oddExt`.
- `halfLine_markovEnergy_add_nonneg_of_riemannHypothesis`: RH ⟹ M(f) + Σ_k f̃(−2k)² + Π(f) ≥ 0 for every half-line test. The converse needs odd negative tests vanishing near 0 (translate the even pole-free negative test u far out: M(u_a − ũ_a) = 2W(u) − 2Σ_ρ e^{(2ρ−1)a}ũ(ρ)²; choose a by the phase of the off-line term) — paper-open, not formalized.
- `productPrimePairing_nonneg` for f ≥ 0. The reflected archimedean kernel of the odd sector is now, in Lean, the sum of squares over the trivial zeros.

## Row 505 — C505: the half-line criterion is an equivalence. Verdict: SURVIVES [P]
- Claim: RH ⟺ for every ε > 0 and every real C² f supported in [ε, ∞): M(f) + Σ_k f̃(−2k)² + Π(f) ≥ 0.
- ⟹ is [L] (row 504). ⟸: the hypothesis gives M(F) ≥ 0 for every odd test F vanishing near 0. For a general odd C² test F, F(x) = O(|x|) near 0; with an even smooth cutoff χ_δ vanishing on (−δ, δ) and equal to 1 off (−2δ, 2δ), F_δ := Fχ_δ is odd, vanishes near 0, and F_δ → F in H¹ (‖Fχ_δ′‖₂² ≍ δ → 0). M is continuous in H¹ on tests of bounded support: the archimedean part is ∫|F̂|²Ω with Ω = O(log|t|), the prime part is a finite sum of L²-continuous pairings ⟨F, τ_{log n}F⟩. Hence M(F) = lim M(F_δ) ≥ 0, and the odd capstone gives RH.
- Alternative Lean route (no continuity): translate the even pole-free negative test u of `exists_even_poleFree_negative_of_not_riemannHypothesis` by a ≫ 0; W and pole-freeness are translation invariant, so M(u_a − ũ_a) = 2W(u) − 2Re Σ_ρ e^{(2ρ−1)a}ũ(ρ)², and a is chosen by the phase of the dominant off-line term. Either route is a moderate Lean project; deferred until an attack needs the converse.

## Row 506 — C506: the nonnegative half-line criterion and its divisor-graph reading. Verdict: SURVIVES [P-sketch] as a criterion, INSIGNIFICANT for the proof
- For f ≥ 0 on [ε, ∞) every term of the odd decomposition has a sign: M(f − f̃) = 2[arch(f) + Σ_k f̃(−2k)² + Π(f) − R(f)] with arch(f) ≥ Ω(0)‖f‖², trivial-zero squares ≥ 0, product pairing Π(f) = Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(f⋆f)(log n) ≥ 0, ratio pairing R(f) = Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(f⋆f̃)(log n) ≥ 0. So RH ⟹ R(f) ≤ arch(f) + Σ_k f̃(−2k)² + Π(f) for all nonnegative half-line tests: products plus archimedean plus trivial-zero squares dominate the ratios.
- Completeness of the nonnegative class: a single narrow nonnegative bump has R = 0 and M(F) > 0, so single bumps never witness ¬RH through M (they can through W, since M − W = 2S² is large); witnesses need ratio content, i.e. several bumps at lags log n. Nonnegative atomic tests f = Σ_j c_j φ(· − log m_j) with c_j ≥ 0 can still concentrate the Dirichlet polynomial Σ c_j m_j^{−it} at a target height γ by Kronecker alignment of γ log m_j mod 2π, so the class is RH-complete in principle; the exact Lean converse is not attempted.
- Atomic reading: R ↔ edges of the divisibility graph on {m_j} with prime-power ratio, weight Λ(m_k/m_j)(m_j/m_k)^{½}; Π ↔ same-prime-power pairs, Σ_p log p(Σ_{m_j = p^r}c_j p^{−r/2})²; trivial-zero squares Σ_k(Σ_j c_j m_j^{−2k−½})²; arch ↔ the regularized Toeplitz form. RH is the sharp divisor-graph inequality R ≤ arch + squares + Π for all nonnegative configurations. Single-prime chains m_j = p^r are harmless (arch ≈ 2‖φ‖²log(1/w) dominates); critical configurations spread over many primes and align at a large height, the uniform sign law again.

## Row 507 — Correction to row 506 and resolution law L507. Verdict: the atomic divisor-graph inequality is TRIVIAL
- For bumps of width w (frequency resolution 1/w), arch(f) = ∫|φ̂|²|D|²Ω ≈ (Σc_j²)(1/(πw))log(1/w) by the mean value of |D|² over the band, while the prime pairings R, Π and the trivial-zero squares are O(1/w)·(configuration constants). Hence M(f − f̃) > 0 automatically as w → 0 at fixed configuration: hyper-narrow atomic tests (w ≪ 1/N², the regime where lattice coincidences are exact) never witness ¬RH. The divisor-graph inequality of row 506 is a trivial consequence of the logarithmic growth of the archimedean symbol, not a criterion.
- Detection of an off-line zero of height γ through M requires (i) resolution w ≲ 1/γ, (ii) Fourier concentration near ±γ so that arch ≈ 2‖φ‖²Ω(γ) ≈ 2‖φ‖²log γ instead of the band average, and (iii) ratio content (M(f) < 0 needs R(f) > arch + squares + Π) with the product main term suppressed (ℓ₁(f) ≈ 0, sign-changing f). Nonnegative tests cannot satisfy (ii) (|f̂| ≤ f̂(0)), so their detection cost is support L ≍ log γ/(β−½) instead of log log γ/(β−½) (row 484), and at that resolution the atoms at log m_j merge: the test is effectively smooth. The nonnegative class is RH-complete but not through its atomic discretization.
- L507 (resolution law): RH content in the Weil/Markov forms lives exactly at resolution ≍ 1/γ, the Nyquist scale of the zeros (law 3); finer resolution is archimedean-dominated and automatically positive, coarser resolution cannot separate the zero from its on-line neighbours. Translation moves odd tests to positivity through the product pairing's growth e^{x₀}ℓ₁(f₀)² unless ℓ₁(f₀) = 0.

## Row 508 — C508: positivity as temperedness of a mean-periodic distribution. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (classical), law L508
- Φ⋆K_W = 0 unconditionally (row 465), so the Weil distribution K_W = A + 2cosh(x/2) − Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(δ_{log n} + δ_{−log n}) is mean-periodic with respect to Riemann's kernel. By Schwartz's spectral synthesis its spectrum is the zero set and its exponential components are e^{(ρ−½)x} with coefficient the multiplicity; an off-line quadruple contributes 4cosh((β−½)x)cos(γx), which grows exponentially. Hence RH ⟺ K_W is a tempered distribution ⟺ K_M + 2cosh(x/2) ∈ 𝒮′ ⟺ Σ_{n≤e^X}Λ(n)n^{−½} − 2e^{X/2} is polynomially bounded in X, the √x prime number theorem. For a tempered mean-periodic distribution the Fourier transform is supported on the real zeros with weights the multiplicities, so positivity is automatic once temperedness holds: in the Weil frame, positivity = growth, and the one negative square of M is exactly the explicitly subtracted non-tempered pole exponential.
- Landau–Gonek (corpus `ZetaFrequencyFamilyZeroSums.exists_abs_re_zeroSum_famPoly_le`): the truncated zero sums Σ_{|Im ρ|≤T}e^{(ρ−½)x} reproduce the prime comb with the negative sign, −(T/2π)Λ(n)n^{−½} at x = log n, unconditionally; the truncation K_{W,T} is PD iff RH holds up to height T, so truncation gains nothing. L508 (growth law): every positivity statement for M on tests reduces to the growth of the PNT remainder measured at scale e^{−x/2}; the Lorentzian/Hodge structure is bookkeeping of the one non-tempered term.

## Row 509 — [L] even-sector counterpart and the two-sided law (same module, rebuilt)
- `markovEnergy_evenExtension_eq`: M(f + f̃) = 2M(f) − 2Σ_k f̃(−2k)² − 2Π(f): the trivial-zero squares and the product pairing enter the even sector with the opposite sign, so the pole's negative square competes exactly with them.
- `halfLine_markovEnergy_ge_of_riemannHypothesis`: RH ⟹ M(f) ≥ Σ_k f̃(−2k)² + Π(f) for half-line tests with ∫f cosh(x/2) = 0 (primitive hyperplane applied to the even extension); with the odd bound, `abs_trivialZero_add_product_le_markovEnergy_of_riemannHypothesis`: |Σ_k f̃(−2k)² + Π(f)| ≤ M(f) on such tests. Under RH the half-line Markov form dominates the trivial-zero squares plus the product pairing in absolute value, a quantitative strengthening of the hyperplane positivity on half-line tests.
- Doob-frame remark (no new survivor): the zero Gram Z is not trace class (each zero contributes ∫Φ/cosh), the Doob jump rates Φ(y)/Φ(x) are super-exponentially confining, and the operator structure adds nothing beyond rows 467–477.

## Row 510 — C510: Liouville/Choquet–Deny for the Φ-balanced Weil distribution and for the Doob chain. Verdict: INSIGNIFICANT
- Φ⋆K_W = 0 says K_W is Φ-balanced: its Φ-weighted average vanishes at every point. For the smoothings D_ε = φ_ε⋆K_W this forces a sign change in every window of width ≍ 1 (Φ positive, super-exponentially concentrated), consistent with the zero spectrum ≥ 14. But the balance equation has eigenvalue 0, not 1, so Choquet–Deny/maximum-principle arguments do not constrain growth: every exponential e^{(ρ−½)x} with Ξ(z_ρ) = 0 is balanced. Growth (L508) is untouched.
- Doob chain: L = L_R − Z with L and the reset generator L_R both Markov with invariant law m, Z the zero Gram. gap(L) ≥ 1 ⟺ Z ≥ 0 ⟺ 𝓔_L ≥ 𝓔_R; Z is not itself a Dirichlet form (the pole term is positive off the diagonal), so no third process realizes the difference; comparison of two Markov generators with equal invariant measure reduces to the Poincaré inequality, which is RH. Nothing passes a wall.

## Row 511 — C511: growth from the balance equation and the sign structure. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (Landau, wall 1)
- Let P be the odd primitive of the Weil distribution on (0, ∞): P(x) = 4sinh(x/2) − Σ_{n≤e^x}Λ(n)n^{−½} + (archimedean primitive, O(log x)), the √x-scaled PNT remainder. Since Φ⋆K_W = 0 and P is odd, Φ⋆P = 0: ∫_0^∞Φ(y)[P(x+y) + P(x−y)]dy = 0 for every x. This is eigenvalue 0, not a mean value property (which would be Φ⋆P = ξ(½)P), so no maximum principle; a monotone exponential e^{ax} is never balanced (∫Φe^{−ay} = ξ(½ − a) > 0), only oscillating growth e^{(β−½)x}cos(γx) is.
- Landau's oscillation theorem gives the one-sided reduction: if P(x) ≤ Cx^A for all large x then RH (the Mellin transform of a one-signed function has a real singularity at its abscissa; −ζ′/ζ(s)/s has none in (½, 1)). So RH ⟸ one-sided polynomial control of the √x remainder, and by the balance identity ⟸ one-sided control of Φ-averaged short-interval remainders. This is wall 1 (Robin/Nicolas/WL one-sided prime counting). Nothing new passes it.

## Row 512 — C512: coercivity and the frame bound. Verdict: INSIGNIFICANT (quantitative exactness)
- Under RH the zeros are a sampling set for tests of support [−L, L]: A(L)‖F‖² ≤ Σ_γ|F̂(γ)|² ≤ B(L)‖F‖². The lower bound is ≍ exp(−πe^{2L}) because the truncated Riemann kernel Φ_L has F̂ ≈ Ξ vanishing at all zeros while ‖Φ_L‖ ≍ 1 (corpus `exists_norm_W_truncatedRiemannKernel_le`): below height 14 there are no zeros, so low-frequency band-limited tests are almost invisible. Coercivity fails exactly along the radical approximants, the quantitative form of the exactness law; no producer.

## Row 513 — C513: mechanism sweep (Speiser, Laguerre, Turán) and the de Branges shift family. Verdict: REDISCOVERED
- Corpus owns: Speiser's direction locally (`SpeiserLocalDisk.deriv_riemannZeta_ne_zero_of_re_zeros`, `SpeiserEventualEquivalence`), the first Laguerre inequality under RH (`XiLaguerrePolya`, `XiLaguerreMarginUnconditional`), Turán/Jensen (`XiTaylorCoefficientsLogConcave`, `RealTaylorCoefficients.splits_jensenPoly_realTaylorCoeff`). No new mechanism there.
- Shift family: E_h(z) := ξ(½ + h − iz) has zeros at z = −γ − i(½ + h − β), so E_h is Hermite–Biehler iff every zero has β < ½ + h. At h = ½ this is unconditional (0 < β < 1), and the Hadamard product ξ(1 − iz) = ξ(1)∏(1 − z/z_n) with no exponential factor gives |ξ(1 + y + it)| > |ξ(1 − y + it)| = |ξ(y + it)| for all y > 0 unconditionally. The de Branges spaces H(E_h) are genuine Hilbert spaces exactly for h > Θ − ½, with norm ∫|F(t)|²/|ξ(½ + h + it)|²dt and orthogonal sampling at the zeros of A_h = ½(Ξ(·+ih) + Ξ(·−ih)) (the cosh family of row 497); as h ↓ 0 the sampling identity degenerates to the Weil form. HB of E_h for all h > 0 ⟺ σ ↦ |ξ(σ + it)| strictly increasing on (½, ∞) ⟺ Re ξ′/ξ > 0 on Re s > ½ (Lagarias), each term (σ − β)/|s − ρ|² of the Hadamard sum being positive iff β < σ. The chain adds a Hilbert-space reading of the quasi-RH ladder and nothing that passes a wall.

## Row 514 — C514: the holomorphic zero sum of a half-line test is archimedean-free. Verdict: SURVIVES, [L]
- For a half-line test f (support in [ε, ∞)), W(f, f̃) = Σ_ρ m_ρ f̂(γ_ρ)² (the holomorphic square sum; f̂(γ_ρ) = f̃(ρ) in Mellin terms) is the full Weil functional of the convolution square f⋆f (supported in (2ε, ∞)): pole term ℓ₀² + ℓ₁², prime term Π(f), archimedean term −Σ_{k≥0} f̃(−2k)². Hence
  Re Σ_ρ m_ρ f̂(γ_ρ)² = ℓ₀² + ℓ₁² − Π(f) − Σ_{k≥0} f̃(−2k)² = ℓ₁(f)² − Π(f) − Σ_{k≥1} f̃(−2k)²,
  with no archimedean integral and no ratio pairing (ℓ₀ is the k = 0 trivial-zero moment). It is the explicit formula in the convolution-square variable, i.e. the Weil distribution K_W paired with f⋆f.
- [L] `W_reflect_re_eq` in `WeilMarkovOddSectorTrivialZeroSquares` (rebuilt, standard axioms), with the bilinear explicit formula `zetaZeroConfig_W_eq_weilFullFunctional_bilinear` (own copy; the opus lane's untracked module has an equivalent lemma under another name), `pole_convSquare_re`, `primes_convSquare_eq`, `arch_convSquare_re` (real part of ∫û²Ω via Re(p²) = (|p+p̄|² − |p−p̄|²)/4 and the even/odd archimedean difference).
- Consequence (paper): for f ≥ 0, Π ≥ 0 gives Σ_ρ f̂(γ_ρ)² ≤ ℓ₁(f)² unconditionally (trivial), and under RH |Σ_ρ f̂(γ_ρ)²| ≤ Σ_ρ|f̂(γ_ρ)|² = W(f), i.e. |ℓ₁² − Π − Σ_{k≥1}f̃(−2k)²| ≤ arch(f) + 2ℓ₀ℓ₁ − R(f): the triangle inequality for the zero data, equivalent to RH by the even/odd capstones. The Hermitian sum needs the archimedean integral and the ratio pairing; the holomorphic sum needs neither.

## Row 515 — C515: damped Weil forms and the one-sided Laplace transform of K_W. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (Lagarias), ladder law restated
- The one-sided Laplace transform of the Weil distribution is the Cauchy transform of the zeros: ∫_0^∞K_W(x)e^{−sx}dx = Σ_ρ 1/(s + ½ − ρ) = (ξ′/ξ)(½ + s), convergent for Re s > Θ − ½. Damping the Weil distribution by e^{−σ|x|} gives the symbol 2Re(ξ′/ξ)(½ + σ + it) = 2Σ_ρ(½ + σ − β)/|½ + σ + it − ρ|², a sum of Lorentzians with sign (½ + σ − β). Hence the damped form W_σ(F) = ∫∫F(x)F(y)K_W(x−y)e^{−σ|x−y|} is PD iff no zero has β ≥ ½ + σ; for σ ≥ ½ this holds unconditionally; RH ⟺ W_σ ≥ 0 for all σ > 0 (Schur product with the PD Laplace kernel in one direction, the limit σ ↓ 0 in the other). This is Lagarias' Re ξ′/ξ > 0 criterion and the quasi-RH ladder in the damping variable: a fixed zero-free strip of any width is equivalent to positivity of one damped form, and is as open as RH in the corpus (ψ(x) − x = O(x^{1−δ}) for a fixed δ > 0 is wall 1 at precision x^{1−δ}). No new mechanism.

## Row 516 — C516: the Weil Hilbert space as a de Branges space; the σ = ½ base of the ladder. Verdict: CIRCULAR / REDISCOVERED
- Unit-weight realization: a de Branges space whose sampling measure at the zeros of A is Σ_γ δ_γ needs A with zeros at the γ's and A′(γ)B(γ) = π. By Hadamard A = e^{g}Ξ with g entire, and |Ξ′(γ)| ≍ e^{−π|γ|/4}, so e^{2g(γ)} must interpolate π/(Ξ′(γ)B₀(γ)) at every zero: the model exists (Weierstrass interpolation) but is as non-constructive as the zero set. L483 confirmed from the de Branges side: the Weil Hilbert space L²(Σδ_γ) has no explicit structure function.
- Transparent form of the one-negative-square law: for half-line f with C_f(λ) := ∫f(x)cos(λx)dx, under RH M(f + f̃)/2 = W(f) + H(f) − 4C_f(i/2)² = 2Σ_γ C_f(γ)² − 4C_f(i/2)², squares at the zeros minus one square at the pole frequency ±i/2 (both poles s = 0, 1 give the same cosine value). RH ⟺ every cosine-square term is a genuine square ⟺ all γ real.
- Base of the ladder: the damped form at σ = ½ has symbol 2Re ξ′/ξ(1 + it) = 2Σ_ρ(1−β)/|1+it−ρ|² > 0 unconditionally; arithmetically Σ_n Λ(n)cos(t log n)/n ≤ ½Re ψ((1+it)/2) + 1/(1+t²) − ½log π for all t, the sharp one-sided twisted Mertens bound (classical via the Hadamard product and 0 < β < 1). Every lower rung σ < ½ is a zero-free strip.

## Row 517 — C517: formalize the σ = ½ base (Re ξ′/ξ > 0 on Re s ≥ 1). Verdict: REDISCOVERED (corpus owns the zero-sum representation)
- Corpus: `ZetaTypicalLocalZeroCount.two_mul_re_logDeriv_xi_eq_tsum_pair` (2Re ξ′/ξ(s) as the sum over zero copies of the pair real parts, off the zeros) and `XiLogDerivReLocality.re_logDeriv_xi_nonneg_of_near_zeros` (only zeros within a unit ordinate window can obstruct positivity, since (Re ρ − ½)² < ¼). Positivity of Re ξ′/ξ for Re s ≥ 1, the σ = ½ damped Weil positivity and the twisted Mertens bound are immediate corollaries; no new Lean target. The Hadamard/Weierstrass product machinery is in `HTGeometry/WeierstrassFactorization*`.

## Row 518 — C518: the holomorphic Gram, bilinear Cauchy–Schwarz, and products of positions. Verdict: INSIGNIFICANT (restatement with a multiplicative reading)
- For half-line tests the holomorphic pairing B(f, g) := Σ_ρ m_ρ f̂(γ_ρ)ĝ(γ_ρ) = ω(f⋆g) is elementary (polarization of row 514): B(f,g) = ℓ₀(f)ℓ₀(g) + ℓ₁(f)ℓ₁(g) − Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(f⋆g)(log n) − Σ_{j≥0} f̃(−2j)g̃(−2j); the Hermitian pairing S(f,g) = ω(f⋆g̃) carries the archimedean integral. Under RH S = AA*, B = AAᵀ for A = [f̂_i(γ)], so the doubled Gram [[S, B],[B̄, S̄]] is PSD and |B(f,g)|² ≤ S(f,f)S(g,g); with g = f this is the triangle inequality of row 514, hence RH ⟺ bilinear Cauchy–Schwarz for all pairs.
- Multiplicative reading: for bumps f at x₀ and g at x₁ the prime term of B counts primes near the PRODUCT e^{x₀}e^{x₁} with the convolution weight, while S(f,f), S(g,g) are translation invariant (autocorrelations). Under ¬RH, B grows like X^{Θ−½} (X = e^{x₀+x₁}) while S stays bounded, so Cauchy–Schwarz for far-apart pairs is exactly the detection of off-line zeros; under RH it is the classical O(√X) bound for smoothed remainders Σ_n Λ(n)κ(log n − log X) − ∫. No unconditional route to the inequality for far-apart pairs exists without the zeros.

## Row 519 — C519: the half Riemann kernel in the odd/even decompositions. Verdict: SURVIVES [P] (exact identity), receiver only
- Take f = Φ·1_{x>0} (half kernel; a smooth cut near 0 changes nothing in the limit). Then f + f̃ = Φ and f − f̃ = Φ sgn. From the two decompositions and M(Φ) = −½ (W(Φ) = 0):
  M(f + f̃) = 2M(f) − 2sq(f) − 2Π(f) = −½,   M(f − f̃) = 2M(f) + 2sq(f) + 2Π(f) = W(Φ sgn) + 8σ₀²,
  where sq(f) = Σ_{k≥0}(∫_0^∞Φ e^{−(2k+½)x}dx)², Π(f) = Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}∫_0^{log n}Φ(x)Φ(log n − x)dx, σ₀ = ∫_0^∞Φ sinh(x/2). Subtracting: 4(sq + Π) = W(Φ sgn) + 8σ₀² + ½, and with (Φ sgn)^(t) = 2i Ξ_s(t), Ξ_s(t) := ∫_0^∞Φ(x)sin(tx)dx the sine transform of Riemann's kernel,
  Σ_γ Ξ_s(γ)² = Π(half-Φ) + Σ_{k≥0}(∫_0^∞Φ e^{−(2k+½)x})² − 2σ₀² − ⅛   (under RH; in general the left side is the odd Weil form of Φ sgn over the zero divisor).
- Consequence: RH ⟹ Π(half-Φ) + Σ_k(∫_0^∞Φe^{−(2k+½)x})² ≥ 2σ₀² + ⅛, a Robin/Nicolas-type necessary inequality between explicit constants built from Riemann's kernel (product pairing of the half kernel, its Laplace values at the trivial zeros, its sinh-moment). It is a receiver: its failure would refute RH, its truth proves nothing, and checking it is numerics. Not pursued; the identity itself is exact and unconditional once W(Φ sgn) is kept on the left.

## Row 520 — mechanism census (transfer operator, Nyman–Beurling, Li). Verdict: all corpus-owned
- Corpus: Mayer transfer operator at s = ½ (`BlindCoreMayerTransferHalf`), ζ as a reciprocal Fredholm determinant (`FredholmDeterminantZeta`), Nyman–Beurling/Báez-Duarte (`NymanBeurlingCriterion`, `BaezDuarteDetectorGram`, `BaezDuarteL2Carrier`), Li/Bombieri–Lagarias coordinates and tests (`WCoordinate`, `SuzukiCorFourSix`). Together with rows 481–519 every named RH mechanism of the literature is either in the corpus or killed at a wall in this lane. Remaining producers must be new combinations, not new names.

## Row 521 — C521: zero curves of Re ξ in the strip and the proportion ladder. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (corpus proportion lane)
- The real zeros a_j(h) of A_h = Re ξ(½ + h + it) (unconditionally real for h ≥ ½, row 497) are joined to the Riemann zeros by the level curves {Re ξ(σ + it) = 0}, ½ ≤ σ ≤ 1; N_real(A_h, T) is non-increasing as h ↓ 0 (collisions remove real pairs) while the total count in the strip is RvM-stable, so RH ⟺ N_real(A_h, T) = N_total(T) for all h > 0. Lower bounds for N_real(A_0, T) are the critical-line proportion theorems (Hardy, Selberg, Levinson; corpus Zeta23 at 67.25%, G75 lane), whose methods are known not to reach 100%. Nothing in this lane adds to the proportion; the exact sector identities do not localize in height.

## Row 522 — C522: exponential half-line tests, Pick kernels and the Carathéodory inequality. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (Nevanlinna–Pick)
- For f_s = e^{−(s−½)x}1_{x>0}, f̂_s(γ_ρ) = 1/(s − ρ), so the Hermitian Gram is the Nevanlinna–Pick kernel of Q = ξ′/ξ: S(f_s, f_{s′}) = Σ_ρ 1/((s−ρ)(s̄′−ρ̄)) = [Q(s) − conj Q(s′)]/(conj s′ − s)·(sign), and the holomorphic Gram is the divided difference B(f_s, f_{s′}) = Σ_ρ 1/((s−ρ)(s′−ρ)) = [Q(s) − Q(s′)]/(s′ − s), elementary in ξ′/ξ (consistent with row 518: for exponential tests the product pairing and trivial-zero sums assemble into ξ′/ξ values). RH ⟺ Pick matrices PSD on Re s > ½ ⟺ Q Nevanlinna there (Lagarias) ⟹ the Carathéodory inequality |(ξ′/ξ)′(s)|·(Re s − ½) ≤ Re(ξ′/ξ)(s), which conversely forces Re ξ′/ξ ≥ 0, so RH ⟺ this differential inequality on Re s > ½. Classical.

## Row 523 — C523: de Bruijn–Newman-smoothed prime remainders. Verdict: INSIGNIFICANT
- With ψ_t = Φe^{tx²} (ψ̂_t = H_t), the ψ_t-smoothed √x-scaled remainder R_t(x) = Σ_ρ H_t(z_ρ)e^{(ρ−½)x} vanishes identically at t = 0 (Φ-smoothed PNT, row 483) and is tempered iff RH for every fixed t > 0 (off-line zeros enter with H_t(z_ρ) ≠ 0 and growth e^{(β−½)x}). ∂_tR_t|_{t=0} is the x²Φ-smoothed remainder, the zero sum with weights −Ξ″(γ_ρ) of no definite sign. The family interpolates between the exactly-cancelled kernel and generic kernels; temperedness for one t > 0 is already RH, so no monotonicity in t helps.

## Row 524 — C524: local-global positivity, partition of unity over places. Verdict: CIRCULAR, law L524
- Weil's form is a sum of local forms ω = Σ_p ω_p + ω_∞ + pole with ω_p(F) = log p ⟨|F̂|², P_p − 1⟩ (Poisson kernel at radius p^{−½}; indefinite, vanishing for p > e^{2L}), ω_∞ = ∫|F̂|²Ω (indefinite, Ω(0) < 0), pole of signature (1,1). A local-global proof would write W = Σ_v Q_v with every Q_v ≥ 0. By L483 each Q_v is then a zero-data form Q_v = Σ_γ w_v(γ)|F̂(γ)|², so a local positivity decomposition is exactly a partition of unity {w_v(γ)}_v over the places at every zero, and the "shares" D_v = Q_v − ω_v of the archimedean and pole terms are determined by the zeros. The only zero-free candidate for the shares is the symbol identity Ω = Σ_p log p(P_p − 1) off the zeros (row 490), which redistributes the archimedean term but whose divergence IS the zero comb. Landau–Gonek gives the dual flat partition: each zero up to height T carries the same share −(1/T)Λ(n)n^{−½} of every prime atom. CIRCULAR.
- L524: a local-global positivity principle for the Weil form would require assigning each zero to the primes; no zero-free assignment exists, consistent with wall 11 (node criticality: removing one prime power destroys positivity).

## Row 525 — C525: the one-sided transform of Φ and the translation mean square. Verdict: FALSE direction / REDISCOVERED
- One-sided transform Ξ₋(z) = ∫_0^∞Φ(x)e^{−izx}dx, so Ξ = Ξ₋ + Ξ₋^# on ℝ and Ξ_s = −Im Ξ₋. If Ξ₋ were Hermite–Biehler, Ξ and Ξ_s would have real interlacing zeros and RH would follow; but one-sided transforms of positive kernels have zeros in both half-planes (the Gaussian gives erfc, with zeros on both sides), so HB of Ξ₋ is strictly stronger than RH and not expected. Corpus has the upper-half-plane continuation law for Ξ′/Ξ (`riemannHypothesis_of_upper_logDeriv_extension`), which is the correct one-sided statement.
- For pole-free u, Π(u_a) = −Re Σ_ρ m_ρ û(γ_ρ)²e^{(2ρ−1)a} − Σ_k tzm_k(u_a)² (row 514 with ℓ₀ = ℓ₁ = 0): the product pairing of the translate is a pure fluctuation whose Fourier expansion in the translation a runs over the zeros; its mean square over a ∈ [0, A] is ½Σ_γ m_γ²|û(γ)|⁴ + o(1) under RH and grows like e^{2(2Θ−1)A} otherwise. This is the L²-mean-square receiver for the √x remainder (walls catalogue: RH ⟺ Π − li ∈ L²(x^{−2}dx)). REDISCOVERED.

## Row 526 — C526: formalize the resolution law for narrow configurations. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (corpus Lévy gain)
- Corpus `SeparatedBumpPrimeBlindPositivity.levy_sub_abs_pole_le_weilArchRe`: for an s-separated configuration of bumps of width δ, (A(0) + e^{−s/2}log((s−δ)/δ))‖f‖² − |2P| ≤ W_∞(f⋆f̃), and `SeparatedBumpWeilPositivity` gives full Weil positivity for separated narrow bumps of span < 2log(1/δ). The archimedean gain log(1/δ) of L507 is exactly this Lévy gain; for half-line configurations in that range the odd and even forms satisfy M(f ∓ f̃) ≥ 0 by W ≥ 0 and the pole bookkeeping, so the resolution law adds no Lean content beyond the corpus. Hyper-narrow positivity is settled; the RH content at resolution ≍ 1/γ remains.

## Row 527 — C527: far-out nonnegative half-line tests are M-positive unconditionally. Verdict: SURVIVES [P], Lean target
- Correction to rows 506–507: for the M-form (not W) nonnegative half-line tests do NOT detect off-line zeros. Exact identity (rows 503, 514): M(f − f̃)/2 = M(f) + sq(f) + Π(f) = W(f) − H(f) + 4S_f². For f ≥ 0 at position x₀ the pole square 4S_f² ≍ e^{x₀}(∫f)² dominates any off-line amplification e^{(2β−1)x₀} (2β − 1 < 1), and near the origin the on-line zeros dominate; so the nonnegative class is RH-complete for W (row 507) but not for M. Witnesses of ¬RH through M need S_f ≈ 0, i.e. sign changes.
- New positivity class (paper proof): let f ≥ 0 be C², supported in [x₀ − 1, x₀ + 1] with f ≥ h on that interval. Then (f⋆f)(y) ≥ h²(2 − |y − 2x₀|) on [2x₀ − 2, 2x₀ + 2], so Π(f) ≥ h²Σ_{e^{2x₀−1} ≤ n ≤ e^{2x₀+1}}Λ(n)n^{−½} ≥ c h² e^{x₀} by Chebyshev/Bertrand (θ(2X) − θ(X) ≥ cX, corpus `Sieve/ChebyshevPNT`). The ratio pairing has lags ≤ 2, so R(f) = Σ_{n ≤ e²}Λ(n)n^{−½}·2g(log n) ≤ C_R‖f‖² (Cauchy–Schwarz), arch(f) ≥ Ω(0)‖f‖² (Lévy form, `archForm_eq_levyEnergy`), sq ≥ 0. Hence M(f − f̃)/2 ≥ c h²e^{x₀} − (C_R + |Ω(0)|)‖f‖² > 0 once e^{x₀} > (C_R + |Ω(0)|)‖f‖²/(c h²). Unconditional; the mechanism is the Chebyshev growth of the product pairing beating the archimedean mass and the short-lag ratio pairing. Consistent with the walls (window sector, far from the null edge). Lean next.

## Row 528 — C527 in Lean: `WeilMarkovFarOutNonnegativeOddPositivity` [L]. Verdict: SURVIVES [L]
- Module `RHTower/WeilMarkovFarOutNonnegativeOddPositivity.lean` (wired after `WeilMarkovOddSectorTrivialZeroSquares`; axioms propext/choice/Quot.sound; lint passed). Capstone `exists_farOut_nonneg_oddExt_markovEnergy_pos (h B) (hh : 0 < h) : ∃ X₀, ∀ f x₀, X₀ ≤ x₀ → C² f → compact → (f = 0 off [x₀−1, x₀+1]) → f ≥ 0 → (f ≥ h on [x₀−1, x₀+1]) → ∫f² ≤ B → 0 < markovEnergy (f − f̃)`.
- Ingredients: `primeSum_le_of_support` (ratio pairing ≤ ratioConst·∫f², ratioConst = 2Σ_{n<8}Λ(n)/√n, lags log n > 2 vanish for n ≥ 8 since e² < 8), `archForm_ge` (arch ≥ 2θ′(0)∫f² from the Lévy form), `sq_le_convSquare` ((f⋆f)(y) ≥ h² for |y − 2x₀| ≤ ½), `exists_psiSum_window` (PNT window: ψ(eX) − ψ(X) ≥ (e−1)X/2 for X ≥ Y₀ from `Sieve.tendsto_sum_vonMangoldt_div`), `productPrimePairing_ge` (Π(f) ≥ e^{−(x₀+¼)}h²(ψ(e^{2x₀+½}) − ψ(e^{2x₀−½}))). Threshold X₀ = max(2, (log Y₀ + ½)/2, ¾ + log max(T,1)) with T = 2(KB+1)/((e−1)h²), K = ratioConst + 2|θ′(0)|.
- Meaning: the odd sector of M has a large unconditional positive cone far from the origin; the one negative square (RH ⟺ ind₋M = 1 on the odd/hyperplane sector, rows 481–503) must live on tests with small sinh-moment S_f and sign changes. The product pairing Π is the only term of M(f − f̃) that grows with position; its PNT growth e^{x₀} is exactly the pole square 4S_f² (row 527 identity), so this class is the shadow of the pole, not of the zeros.

## Row 529 — C529: thin nonnegative half-line tests are M-positive at every position. Verdict: SURVIVES [L] `WeilMarkovThinNonnegativeOddPositivity`
- Statement [L] `thin_nonneg_oddExt_markovEnergy_ge`: f ≥ 0 real C² supported in [x₀, x₀ + w], x₀ > 0, w ≤ thinWidth := exp(−e^{1/2}(2 + 2|θ′(0)|)) ⟹ M(f − f̃) ≥ 2∫f². Strict version `thin_nonneg_oddExt_markovEnergy_pos`. Wired after `WeilMarkovFarOutNonnegativeOddPositivity`; axioms standard; lint passed (the lint error `MarkovThreeStageCountertermAndSpectralGapRigidityPBAP` is another lane's unwired module).
- Mechanism: odd decomposition M(f − f̃) = 2[A(f) − P(f)] + 2Σtzm² + 2Π(f); P(f) = 0 for w < log 2 (`primeSum_eq_zero_of_width_lt`: every lag log n ≥ log 2 moves the support off itself); Π ≥ 0, tzm² ≥ 0 for f ≥ 0; Lévy gain `levyEnergy_ge_of_width`: for x > w the copies f(u), f(u+x) are disjoint so the inner integral is 2‖f‖², and ω(x) = e^{−x/2}/(1 − e^{−2x}) ≥ e^{−1/2}/(2x) on (0,1] (`archLevyDensity_ge`), giving A(f) ≥ (2θ′(0) + e^{−1/2}log(1/w))‖f‖².
- Compared with the corpus `SeparatedBumpWeilPositivity` (W-positivity for separated narrow bumps, span < 2log(1/δ)): the odd sector removes the span restriction, because the interaction between the bumps f and −f̃ passes only through Π ≥ 0 and the trivial-zero squares. Positivity cone of the odd M-form now: (i) fat nonnegative far out (row 528, PNT), (ii) thin nonnegative anywhere (row 529, Lévy gain), (iii) hyper-narrow separated configurations (corpus). A negative direction of M|odd must be sign-changing, or nonnegative of intermediate width (thinWidth < w) at bounded position (x₀ < X₀(h,B)); the intermediate nonnegative gap is a PNT-with-error question (positions x₀ ∈ [log 2, X₀]), finite in extent for each (h, w, B).

- C530a (PNT main term absorbed into the pole): pole − P_main = ∫∫f(t)f(s)e^{−|t−s|/2} = (1/2π)∫|f̂|²/(¼ + r²): W = (1/2π)∫|f̂|²[Ω + 1/(¼+r²)] − P_fluct with P_fluct the pairing of f⋆f̃ with the √-normalised PNT remainder measure. - C530b (translation dichotomy): for pole-free half-line φ (∫φe^{t/2} = 0), M(φ_{x₀} − φ̃_{x₀}) = 2W(φ) + 2e^{−x₀}ℓ₀(φ)² − 2H(φ_{x₀}) with H(φ_{x₀}) = Re Σ_ρ m_ρ φ̂(γ_ρ)²e^{(1−2ρ)x₀} an exponential polynomial in x₀; RH ⟺ H bounded along every orbit ⟺ sup_{x₀} H(φ_{x₀}) ≤ W(φ). REDISCOVERED (L508 temperedness; ZAL growth receivers).
- C530c (multi-bump avoidance class): Σa_ib_i with thin bumps b_i at positions x_i whose sums and differences avoid the lattice {log n} within the width have M(F) > 0 (prime terms vanish, Lévy gain dominates by Gershgorin). TRUE but INSIGNIFICANT: without prime terms M = A > 0 for thin tests; witnesses must resonate with the lattice — trivial.
- C530d (full nonnegative half-line cone M-positive unconditionally): TRUE under RH; unconditionally reduces to PNT-with-explicit-error at bounded scales for nonnegative weights f⋆f, i.e. a finite region of (position, width) with explicit small-prime data; not RH-content, not Lean-worthy (numerical theatre). Near the origin (support in [0, ¼]) the margin comes from the trivial-zero squares and the high-frequency archimedean part (Connes–Consani aperture region, corpus SuzukiPositiveAperture lane).
- C530e (Krein–Langer κ = 1 kernels): M's translation-invariant kernel has exactly one negative square ⟺ its only non-real frequencies are ±i/2 ⟺ RH. Reformulation; extension problems indeterminate (L488). REDISCOVERED.

Lean target: Ω(r) ≤ Ω(0) + m₂r² with m₂ = ∫_0^∞x²ω(x)dx ≤ 18.

## Row 531 — L531 archimedean indefiniteness law [L] `WeilMarkovArchimedeanWideTestNegativity`. Verdict: SURVIVES [L] (structural no-go)
- [L] `gammaBracket_le_quadratic`: Ω(r) ≤ Ω(0) + m₂r² with m₂ = ∫_0^∞x²ω(x)dx (`levySecondMoment`), `levySecondMoment_le : m₂ ≤ 18` (x²ω ≤ (x² + x/2)e^{−x/2} via 1 − e^{−2x} ≥ 2x/(1+2x); Gamma integrals 16 + 2). Proof: Lévy–Khintchine θ′(r) − θ′(0) = ∫ω(1 − cos rx) and 1 − cos y ≤ y²/2.
- [L] `archForm_le`: A(g) ≤ 2θ′(0)‖g‖² + m₂·(1/2π)∫r²|ĝ|² (Plancherel `integral_norm_paperFT_sq`, decay `norm_paperFT_le_decay` for integrability). [L] `exists_wide_archForm_le`: ∃κ > 0, (1/2π)∫r²|ĝ|² ≤ κ‖g‖² ⟹ A(g) ≤ θ′(0)‖g‖² < 0. [L] `exists_wide_nonneg_markovEnergy_le`: for g ≥ 0 wide, M(g) ≤ θ′(0)‖g‖² (ratio pairing ≥ 0, `primeSum_nonneg`). [L] `wide_nonneg_productPairing_ge_of_riemannHypothesis`: RH ⟹ Σ_k tzm_k(g)² + Π(g) ≥ −θ′(0)‖g‖² for wide nonnegative half-line g.
- Wired after `WeilMarkovThinNonnegativeOddPositivity`; axioms standard; lint passed (the lint error `MarkovExactFirstCellCosLogIntegralAndTwentyThreeRatioPBAP` is another lane's unwired module).
- Meaning (L531): the archimedean form is negative on every wide test (Fourier H¹ ≤ κ‖g‖²), an infinite-dimensional set; the ratio pairing is nonnegative on nonnegative tests; so on wide nonnegative tests the half-line Markov form M = A − P is strictly negative and the odd-sector positivity M(g − g̃) ≥ 0 (RH) is carried entirely by Π + Σtzm². Hence the "exactly one negative square" of M on the full test space is a cancellation between the infinitely indefinite A and the infinitely indefinite −P (their difference has inertia (1, ∞) under RH), not "positive A minus a small prime perturbation". No proof strategy of the form "A dominates P" exists; the positivity must come from the prime side (Π), i.e. from PNT-type lower bounds for the product pairing — which is where rows 528/529 found their unconditional cones (PNT window growth, or P = 0 with the Lévy gain).

## Row 532 — C532a–f: mechanisms for M_odd ≥ 0 in the half-line frame. Verdicts: FALSE / REDISCOVERED / CIRCULAR
Frame (exact): for half-line f, Q(f) := M(f) + Σ_k tzm_k² + Π(f) = ∫∫ f(x)f(y) q(x,y) with off-diagonal kernel q(x,y) = J(x+y) − J(x−y), J(z) = ω(|z|) + Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}δ(|z| − log n) (the Dirichlet reflection of the full-line jump kernel; ω(x+y) = Σ_k e^{−(2k+½)(x+y)} is exactly the trivial-zero-square kernel). The arch part ω(|x−y|) − ω(x+y) ≥ 0 is Markov on the half line (ω decreasing); the only anti-Markov part is the product atoms x + y = log n.
- C532a (odd Barta/Allegretto–Piepenbrink with h > 0 on (0,∞), bounding the anti-Markov term by (v(x)−v(y))² ≤ 2v(x)² + 2v(y)²): the resulting sufficient condition is exactly K_M ⋆ (h + h̃) ≥ 0, a positive EVEN supersolution of M, which would give M ≥ 0 on all tests — contradicting M(Φ) = −½. FALSE (the crude bound converts the odd problem into the false even one). Lesson: in the odd sector the anti-Markov term must be kept as a cancellation, never bounded by squares.
- C532b (rearrangement: Q(f) ≥ Q(|f|), then use the nonnegative cones of rows 528–529): Q(f₊ − f₋) − Q(f₊ + f₋) = −4Q(f₊,f₋) and Q(f₊,f₋) = −∫∫f₊f₋[J(x−y) − J(x+y)]; arch part favourable (ω(x+y) ≤ ω(|x−y|)), product atoms unfavourable whenever supp f₊ + supp f₋ meets log n off the ratio lattice. FALSE at product resonances (thin bumps at x₁ + x₂ = log 2 beat |f| for widths up to e^{−10.5}).
- C532c (gauge/stoquastic basis s(x) ∈ {±1} making all off-diagonal entries ≤ 0): the arch kernel is a density on every pair, forcing s constant; product atoms remain positive. FALSE (frustrated signed graph).
- C532d (Doob frame by Φ): M(Φv) = E_J^Φ(v) − ∫v²Φcosh(x/2) with E_J^Φ a genuine nonnegative jump form (no anti-Markov terms on the full line); RH ⟺ Poincaré constant of the Riemann–Doob jump process on odd functions ≥ ½, sharp (equality approached on the odd near-radical family). Equivalently: the exponentials orthogonal to 1 in L²(Φdx) are exactly e^{(ρ−½)x} (corpus `RiemannKernelGroundStateZeroIdentity`), so RH ⟺ every non-unitary character is non-orthogonal to 1 in L²(Φdx) = Pólya's real-zero problem for the Fourier transform of Φ. REDISCOVERED (row 467 + corpus ground-state lane + Pólya). Sharp-gap tools (Bakry–Émery, intertwining) need an exactly solvable structure; the prime jumps are not one.
- C532e (Φ′-Doob odd frame, signed network with effective-resistance domination instead of pointwise degree): at the cusp (x → ∞) the PNT main terms of the good ratio edges and the bad product edges between x and y ∈ (0,1) give net density −2H(x)H(y)e^{x/2}sinh(y/2), which against the potential ½H(x)sinh(x/2) balances EXACTLY at leading order (¼ − 2∫_0^∞(−Φ′)sinh(y/2) = ¼ − 2·⅛ = 0). So cusp positivity is a pure PNT-remainder statement. CIRCULAR (wall 1; row 476 uniform sign law).
- C532f (Selberg-cusp analogy: gap ≥ ¼ for SL₂(ℤ) cusp forms follows from x-Fourier modes and y ≥ √3/2; transfer to the odd Doob gap): ζ enters SL₂(ℤ)\ℍ only through Eisenstein constant terms (free-family law, row 364); the discrete spectrum is ζ-free. FALSE transfer.
- Interrogation (what stronger conjecture survives these kills): every kill is either a pointwise/local domination (destroyed by exactness: equality is approached on an infinite-dimensional near-radical family) or a PNT-main-term balance whose remainder is wall 1. A surviving mechanism must (i) be exact (an identity, not an inequality with loss), (ii) treat the product atoms x + y = log n as a cancellation partner of the ratio atoms |x − y| = log n, i.e. use the FE involution ι : x ↦ −x together with the multiplicative lattice, and (iii) fail for ζ(s−a)ζ(s+a) (non-tempered weights Λ(n)(n^a + n^{−a})). Candidate for row 533: a product–ratio exchange identity built from Λ = μ ⋆ log and reflections about log d / 2.

## Row 533 — C533: pole shielding of nonnegative odd tests. Verdict: SURVIVES [P], INSIGNIFICANT for ζ (configuration law)
- Zero-side form of the odd sector (exact): for real half-line f and S(w) := ∫_0^∞ f(x) sinh(wx) dx, M(f − f̃) = 8S(½)² − 4Σ_ρ m_ρ Re[S(ρ − ½)²] (Wsummand of an odd test = −4m S(ρ−½)²; the pole is a "zero at w = ½ with weight −2"). On-line zeros give −4Re S² = 4(∫f sin γx)² ≥ 0.
- Lemma: for 0 ≤ δ ≤ ½ and x ≥ 0, sinh(δx) ≤ 2δ sinh(x/2) (t ↦ t coth(tx) increasing). Hence for f ≥ 0: Re[S(δ+iγ)²] ≤ (∫f sinh(δx)cos γx)² ≤ 4δ²S(½)².
- Theorem [P]: M(f − f̃) ≥ 8S(½)²(1 − 2D) for every nonnegative half-line f, where D = Σ_ρ m_ρ(Re ρ − ½)². So nonnegative odd tests are blind to every off-line configuration of total squared depth D ≤ ½ (one quadruple of depth δ ≤ 1/(2√2) is invisible). The constant 2δ is asymptotically sharp for tests concentrated near the origin, but there the on-line zeros dominate (resolution law), so the true blindness threshold is larger.
- For ζ, D < ∞ is unknown unconditionally (zero-density estimates do not bound Σ(β−½)²), so no unconditional consequence. Cluster check: the corpus `OffLineClusterWeilUnbounded` configuration (multiplicity ≍ log γ at depth ¼) has D = ∞, consistent. Not formalized (configuration-level, no RH content).

## Row 534 — the half-line trivial-zero criterion, both directions [L] `WeilMarkovHalfLineTrivialZeroCriterion`. Verdict: SURVIVES [L]
- [L] `riemannHypothesis_iff_forall_halfLine_trivialZeroSquare_nonneg`: RH ⟺ for every real C² compactly supported f with f = 0 on (−∞, ε), ε > 0: M(f) + Σ_k(∫f e^{−(2k+½)x})² + Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(f⋆f)(log n) ≥ 0. [L] `riemannHypothesis_iff_forall_halfLine_archForm_trivialZeroSquare_nonneg`: RH ⟺ P(f) ≤ A(f) + Σ_k(∫f e^{−(2k+½)x})² + Π(f) on all half-line tests (ratio-prime pairing dominated by archimedean form + trivial-zero squares + product-prime pairing). Axioms standard; wired after `WeilMarkovArchimedeanWideTestNegativity`; lint passed except the other lane's unwired `MarkovExactFirstCellCosLogIntegralAndTwentyThreeRatioPBAP`.
- Converse mechanism (closes the [P] of row 505 without H¹-continuity): `integral_W_oddShift_mul_weight` generalizes the corpus Φ-averaging of odd shifts O_h = (u(·−h/2) − u(·+h/2))/2 to any continuous weight g with ‖g(h)‖e^{|h|/2} ≤ Ce^{−|h|/2} and ∫g e^{±iγ_ρ h} = 0; the translated kernel Φ(h − h₀) qualifies (`integral_riemannPhiKernel_shift_gammaOf`, transform e^{iγh₀}Ξ(γ)). So ∫ W(O_h)Φ(h − h₀)dh = ½(∫Φ)W(u) < 0 for every h₀, while |W(O_h)| ≤ e^{|h|/2}Σ‖W_ρ(u)‖ and Φ ≤ C e^{−2|t|} make the part over h ≤ 2b at most T e^{−2(h₀−2b)}. For large h₀ some h > 2b has W(O_h) < 0, and for h > 2b, O_h = f − f̃ with f = u(· − h/2)/2 supported in [h/2 − b, ∞).
- Meaning: the half-line frame is now an exact Lean target. Proving the displayed inequality unconditionally = RH. Unconditional parts in Lean: far-out fat nonnegative f (row 528), thin nonnegative f (row 529); impossibility of "A dominates P" (row 531).

## Row 535 — C535: is odd positivity a property of the spectral density on the line? Verdict: FALSE (structural law L535, STATUS MUST-1507)
- M(F) depends only on k = F⋆F̃, i.e. on |F̂|² on ℝ. The Hilbert transform HΦ of Riemann's kernel is real, odd, decays like Ξ(0)/(πx), and has |(HΦ)^|² = Ξ², so k_{HΦ} = k_Φ and M(HΦ) = M(Φ) = −½ < 0. So "M ≥ 0 on odd tests" (⟺ RH) is false on the odd tests of polynomial decay; it is a statement about the decay class e^{|x|/2} (strip analyticity of F̂ in |Im z| ≤ ½).
- Exact separation: for odd compactly supported F, k̂(z) = −F̂(z)² = b(z)² with b = −iF̂ an odd real entire function, so k̂(±i/2) = b(i/2)²·(sign) ≤ 0 (pole term −2S² ≤ 0), while k̂_Φ(±i/2) = ¼ > 0; convergence in L¹(e^{|x|/2}dx) controls k̂ on the closed strip, so k_Φ is not a limit of odd autocorrelations. Even compactly supported F give k̂ = a(z)² with a even real entire, k̂(±i/2) ≥ 0.
- Consequence for proof shape: RH ⟺ W(b²) + 2b(i/2)²-type pole term ≥ 0 for every odd real entire PW function b; any argument that only uses k̂ ≥ 0 on ℝ and k̂(0) = 0 proves a false statement. Positivity must use the parity of the entire square root b of k̂ (equivalently the e^{|x|/2} decay of F).
- Even-sector corollary [P] (not formalized): the even shifts E_h = (u(·−h/2) + u(·+h/2))/2 of a pole-free even negative u satisfy W(E_h) = Σ_ρ cos²(γ_ρh/2)W_ρ(u), and the same translated-Φ averaging gives ∫W(E_h)Φ(h−h₀)dh = ½(∫Φ)W(u) < 0; for h > 2b, E_h = f + f̃ with f = u(·−h/2)/2 and ∫f cosh(x/2) = 0. Hence RH ⟺ |Σ_k(∫f e^{−(2k+½)x})² + Π(f)| ≤ M(f) for all half-line f with ∫f cosh(x/2) = 0 (both directions; the odd witness also has ∫f cosh(x/2) = 0).
- STATUS entries added: MUST-1506 (row 534), CANNOT-558 (row 531), F-route-tt-528/529, CANNOT-559 (row 532), MUST-1507 (row 535).

## Row 536 — C536a–e: non-certificate mechanisms in the half-line frame. Verdicts: CIRCULAR / REDISCOVERED / FALSE; one structural fact
- C536a (de la Vallée Poussin 3-4-1 in the odd sector): the classical zero-free argument uses tests k ≥ 0 pointwise so that Λ ≥ 0 makes the prime side signed, then W ≤ A + pole. In the odd sector the pole has the helpful sign (W = A − P − 2S²), but **every odd autocorrelation has mean zero** (k̂(0) = −F̂(0)² = 0 since F̂(0) = ∫F = 0), so k_F ≥ 0 pointwise forces F = 0. The prime side can only be signed by placing the negative part of k_F between the logs of prime powers; at support length ≳ log γ the prime-power logs are denser than the oscillation period 2π/γ, so this is possible only for prime-blind or prime-positive odd tests, i.e. odd PBAP (other lane; odd PBAP ⟹ RH needs odd uniqueness, rows 399–405, STATUS MUST-1505). FACT [P]: there is no odd-sector version of the 3-4-1 mechanism without blindness. CIRCULAR (reduces to PBAP).
- C536b (Rankin/Deligne tensor-power trick via convolution powers f^{⋆n}): (f^{⋆n})^ = f̂^n, so an off-line zero would dominate Q(f^{⋆n}) if |f̂(γ−iδ)| > max(|ℓ₁(f)|, on-line |f̂(γ)|); a contradiction needs a prime-side bound on Q(f^{⋆n}) with growth below |f̂(γ−iδ)|^{2n}, i.e. √x cancellation (wall 1). The trick works for Deligne because symmetric powers are new L-functions; for ζ every tensor power is ζ again. FALSE as a mechanism.
- C536c (phase retrieval): M depends only on |F̂|² on ℝ; W = M + pole depends on the phase only through F̂(±i/2). Zero flips a ↦ ā of F̂ preserve M and PW type. Under RH the admissible phases of |Ξ|² are translations only (Ξ has no non-real zeros to flip), so the pole value ½ is forced and W(Φ) = 0. Flipping an off-line quadruple of Ξ also preserves the pole value (the factor ∏(ā²+¼)/(a²+¼) over {a, −ā} is 1). INSIGNIFICANT.
- C536d (Selberg trace formula of SL₂(ℤ) as a positivity source): ζ zeros enter only through the scattering determinant φ(s) = ξ(2s−1)/ξ(2s) as resonances; the discrete-spectrum positivity is ζ-free. REDISCOVERED (free-family law, row 364).
- C536e (Castelnuovo–Severi dictionary): W ≥ 0 ⟺ −M(F) ≤ 2ℓ₀(F)ℓ₁(F), the analogue of D² ≤ 2d₁d₂ with the two pole functionals as the two fibre degrees. REDISCOVERED (Weil's own framing); its proof needs the surface (wall 5). Corpus function-field calibration (rh-goal-loop-opus row 688): Markovianity of the pole-free form on (log q)ℤ does not give index one for curves either.

## Row 537 — sector duality of pole and trivial zeros in the half-line frame. Verdict: SURVIVES [P] as an exact law (L537); Phase 6/7 posted
- With L_f(a) = ∫f e^{−ax}, L₊ = L_f(½), L₋ = L_f(−½), T = Σ_{k≥1}L_f(2k+½)² (the true trivial zeros s = −2, −4, …; the k = 0 term of the Lévy kernel is the Gamma pole at s = 0, i.e. the pole of π^{−s/2}Γ(s/2)ζ(s) at s = 0), and X = A(f) + 2L₋L₊:
  W(f − f̃)/2 = (X − P) + (Π + T − L₋²),   W(f + f̃)/2 = (X − P) − (Π + T − L₋²),
  where X − P = W(f) is the Weil functional of the half-line test itself. The k = 0 Lévy reflection term L₊² cancels against the s = 0 pole square in both sectors; what remains is the s = 1 pole square L₋² against the true trivial-zero squares T and the product-prime pairing Π.
- Hence RH ⟺ |Π(f) + T(f) − L₋(f)²| ≤ W(f) for every half-line f [P; odd half [L] row 534, even half via even shifts as in row 535]. The left side is archimedean-free (it equals −Re Σ_ρ m F(ρ)², F the Laplace transform; corpus row 514 `W_reflect_re_eq`): RH ⟺ |Σ_ρ F(ρ)²| ≤ Σ_ρ F(ρ)F(1−ρ).
- Blind specialisations: odd PBAP reads X + T ≥ L₋² and even PBAP reads X + L₋² ≥ T on blind half-line tests, so the pole at s = 1 and the true trivial zeros play mirror roles: the pole hurts the odd sector and helps the even one, the trivial zeros the reverse. A test blind in both sectors (r_f(log n) = p_f(log n) = 0) satisfies both iff X ≥ |L₋² − T|.
- Verdict for the RH core: reformulation (receiver); it locates the arithmetic of both sectors in the single elementary functional Π + T − L₋².

The odd uniqueness problem now needs only the odd blind null kernel (OPEN CORE 1 / row 1231 target) and the critical-zero rigidity step.

## Row 539 — C539: odd spectral gap of the Riemann–Doob jump process by coupling. Verdict: FALSE as a proof; exact criticality law L539 [P]
- Normalisation: with m(dx) = Φ(x)cosh(x/2)dx and E^Φ(v) = ½∫∫(v(x)−v(y))²Φ(x)Φ(y)J(x−y), M(Φv) = E^Φ(v) − ‖v‖²_{L²(m)}; E^Φ is the Dirichlet form of the jump process with rates q(x, dy) = Φ(y)J(x−y)dy / cosh(x/2) on L²(m). RH ⟺ E^Φ(v) ≥ ‖v‖²_{L²(m)} for odd v (odd spectral gap ≥ 1).
- Cusp criticality (exact): the Φ-smoothed explicit formula K_M ⋆ Φ = −cosh(x/2) (row 483; the zeros drop out because Φ̂ = Ξ vanishes on them) gives the prime jump rate into the core Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}(Φ(x−log n) + Φ(x+log n))/cosh(x/2) = 1 + [2θ′(0)Φ(x) + ∫ω(|x−z|)(Φ(x) − Φ(z))dz]/cosh(x/2), and the Lévy jumps into the core contribute the compensating ∫ω(|x−z|)Φ(z)dz/cosh(x/2). So the rate of returning from x → ∞ to the core tends to exactly 1 = the required gap: the odd essential spectrum threshold equals the RH constant, and an off-line quadruple is an odd bound state below the threshold (rows 466–468, exactness law, made explicit).
- Coupling attempt: mirror coupling (Y = −X) plus maximal coalescence. The coalescence rate from (x, −x) is ∫Φ(z)min(J(x−z), J(x+z))dz / cosh(x/2). The Lévy part gives ≥ ∫Φ(z)ω(|x|+|z|)dz / cosh(x/2), which is infinite at x = 0 but decays like e^{−|x|} at the cusp; the prime atoms coalesce only on the product resonance nm = e^{2x} (x − log n = log m − x), a null set. So sign memory is lost only after the mirrored pair returns to the core, at rate exactly 1 at the cusp: the coupling bound is critical and cannot certify the gap. The obstruction is again the product atoms x + y = log n (row 532), now as the coalescence condition.
- Verdict: the Doob/coupling route is exactly critical at the cusp by the Φ-smoothed PNT; a proof must control the next-order (PNT-remainder) correction to the return rate, which is wall 1.

- Problem: odd tests see the weight deviation D_w = Σ_{n≥2} w(n)n^{−½}(δ_{log n} + δ_{−log n}) only through Φ′ ⋆ H = −Φ ⋆ H′, H odd; H′ ranges over the even mean-zero tests, so the pairing determines G_w = D_w ⋆ Φ only up to an additive constant (the invisible atom at r = 0). ## Row 541 — C541a/b: odd majorant uniqueness for summable deviations; balayage existence of Weil-positive weights. Verdicts: REDISCOVERED / CIRCULAR
- C541a (odd-positive λ = Λ + w with Σ|w(n)|n^{−½} < ∞ forces w = 0): corpus `vonMangoldt_add_eq_zero_of_symbol_atom_odd` (`WeilOddConeHalfPoleRigidity`) already covers every deviation with a continuous symbol, of either sign. REDISCOVERED.

Temperedness of Û is RH-strength, so this is a restatement of OPEN CORE 1, not a route.
