Corpus-known in substance (MUST "RH ⟺ PBAP and cone ⊆ {Λ}"); no new STATUS entry.

## Row 542 — C542: Riemann–Roch for the archimedean-minus-prime form (Hodge-index proof shape). Verdict: FALSE; structural law L542 [P]
- Dictionary: −M is the intersection form, Φ the ample class (−M(Φ) = ½, M(Φ, F) = −∫F cosh(x/2)), primitive classes = {∫F cosh(x/2) = 0}. Effective cone := nonnegative functions. Checks: (i) effective E ≠ 0 has ∫E cosh(x/2) > 0 (ample is positive on effective); (ii) M is Markov on the full line (K_M(z) ≤ 0 for z ≠ 0: −Lévy density and −prime atoms), so disjointly supported effective E₁, E₂ have −M(E₁, E₂) ≥ 0 (effective classes without common component meet nonnegatively). Linear equivalence := adding the M-radical, which contains {Φ ⋆ h : ĥ(±i/2) = 0} (D ⋆ Φ = 0 unconditionally, D the Weil distribution).
- C542 (Riemann–Roch step): M(F) < 0 ⟹ ±(F + Φ ⋆ h) ≥ 0 for some h with ĥ(±i/2) = 0. This would give RH by the Hodge-index argument (primitive + effective ⟹ zero).
- Kill (even under RH): a nonnegative G has, for every λ > 0 and |c| ≤ 1, ⟨G, λe^{x/2} + λ^{−1}e^{−x/2} − 2Re(c e^{iγx})⟩ ≥ 0, hence |Ĝ(γ_ρ)| ≤ √(G₊G₋) at every zero (G_± = ∫G e^{±x/2}); this is an ℓ^∞ bound on the class data, invariant under the radical. Under RH, M(F) = Σ_ρ|F̂(γ_ρ)|² − 2F₊F₋, so a class with a single nonzero zero value |F̂(γ₁)| = 1.2√(F₊F₋) has M(F) = −0.56F₊F₋ < 0, is not represented by any nonnegative function, and −F has (−F)₊ < 0. FALSE.
- L542 [P]: in the M-quotient (data (F̂(γ_ρ))_ρ, F₊, F₋), the effective cone is cut out by ℓ^∞ conditions (Bochner + log-convexity of Ĝ(iy)), while M is an ℓ² quantity; convolution powers F^{⋆n} send M(F) < 0 to M(F^{⋆n}) = Σ|F̂(γ)|^{2n} − 2(F₊F₋)^n, which turns positive for n ≥ 2 in the example: the form is not homogeneous under the multiplicative structure, so there is no Riemann–Roch growth h⁰(nD) ~ n²D²/2 to exploit. The Hodge-index shape needs a genuinely quadratic multiplicative structure (the surface; wall 5).
- Interrogation: what survives? A proof via effectivity needs an effective cone whose dual in the zero-data quotient is ℓ²-shaped (Hilbertian), i.e. an effective notion defined by a positive-definite condition rather than pointwise positivity. Positive-definite functions as "effective": k = F⋆F̃ has k̂ = |F̂|² ≥ 0 on ℝ but the pole data k̂(±i/2) are not sign-controlled (row 535). Next: test "effective = autocorrelation of a half-line function" (half-line Bochner cone).

## Row 543 — C543: index-one approximation (lower semicontinuity of the index) and the parity weakening. Verdict: REDISCOVERED
- Observation: the negative index is lower semicontinuous under pointwise convergence of forms (a negative-definite finite-dimensional subspace of the limit stays negative definite nearby). Weil forms on compactly supported tests see finitely many weights, so λ_k(n) → Λ(n) for every n gives W_{λ_k} → W_Λ pointwise. Each off-line quadruple contributes exactly a (2,2) block (data a = f̂(γ), b = f̂(γ̄), form 4Re(a b̄)), so ind(W_Λ) = 2q and ind(M_Λ) = 1 + 2q (q = number of off-line quadruples with multiplicity; even sector 1 + q, odd sector q).
- C543 (parity weakening): RH ⟺ W_Λ ≥ 0 on SOME codimension-one subspace ⟺ M_Λ ≥ 0 on some codimension-two subspace (pure linear algebra: a negative-definite plane meets every hyperplane); equivalently RH ⟸ ∃ λ_k → Λ pointwise with ind(W_{λ_k}) ≤ 1.
- Kill: corpus `FECarrier.fe_quadruple_kappa_two` (κ₋ = 2 per off-line zero), `SeamPolarizationWeld.polarized_negIndex_even`, `XiGaussianHeatVerticalGramIndex.heatGram_negativeIndex_eq_card`, and the pole-free criteria (`exists_even_poleFree_negative_of_not_riemannHypothesis`, `exists_odd_poleFree_negative_of_not_riemannHypothesis`) already contain the parity law and its codimension-two corollary. Approximants with ind ≤ 1: truncations Λ·1_{n≤k} have infinite index (the translation-invariant symbol Ω − 2Σ_{n≤k}Λ(n)n^{−½}cos(t log n) is negative on a set of positive measure); finite index forces the λ-distribution to be the transform of a positive measure plus finitely many exponentials, i.e. λ is the von Mangoldt function of a zeta-like object; the heat family Ξ_t is Hurwitz/de Bruijn (corpus `DeBruijnHeatFlowRealZeros`). REDISCOVERED.
- Phase 6/7 posted after this row (early): rows 541–543 produced one small new law (L542) and no frontier move; next cycle studies the modular-arc minimum-principle machinery that reduced RH to PBAP, aiming at a minimum principle for PBAP itself rather than another reformulation of the M-form.

## Row 544 — C544: sum–difference (Buja–Logan–Reeds–Shepp) form and copositivity of the odd kernel. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (exact reformulation), INSIGNIFICANT as a mechanism
- Free representative: every odd test is F = g − g̃ with g ≥ 0 (g = Fχ + h, χ(x) + χ(−x) = 1, h even with h ≥ |Fχ|; minimal choice: F₊ on the right, reflected F₋ on the left). Hence RH ⟺ the kernel N(x,y) = K_M(x−y) − K_M(x+y) is copositive on ℝ ⟺ E K_M(X−Y) ≥ E K_M(X+Y) for iid X, Y with any law in the test class; multiplicatively, ratios U/V of iid positive variables must out-weigh products UV at the prime powers beyond what the archimedean difference absorbs.
- Check: E K(X−Y) − E K(X+Y) = ∫K̂(t)·2(Im φ(t))² dt, φ the characteristic function, so the inequality holds for every positive-definite K and for every negative-definite ψ = −K (the BLRS inequality E|X+Y| ≥ E|X−Y|); for K_M it is exactly K̂_M ≥ 0 on the odd sector. Decomposing K_M = D − 2cosh(·/2): E K_M(X−Y) − E K_M(X+Y) = [E D(X−Y) − E D(X+Y)] + 8(E sinh(X/2))², the pole square appearing as an iid identity.
- Why no gain: N(g) depends only on g − g̃, so a PSD-plus-nonnegative (SPN) certificate N = P + Z must have Z = 0 (Z vanishes on all nonnegative even g) — copositivity equals positive semidefiniteness here, with no slack. The frustration of the half-line frame (row 532) reappears as the indefinite sign of N.

## Row 545 — C545: Lefschetz principle (signature constancy along nondegenerate paths) via the window path. Verdict: REDISCOVERED (Krein first-crossing)
- Principle (Adiprasito–Huh–Katz / Elias–Williamson / Krein): the signature is locally constant on nondegenerate forms, so positivity at one point plus nondegeneracy along a path gives positivity at the end. With the window path L ↦ W_L (tests supported in [−L, L]; compact resolvent because the symbol grows like log|t|; W_L > 0 for small L): RH ⟺ for every L the truncated Weil equation 1_{(−L,L)}(D ⋆ f) = 0, supp f ⊆ [−L, L], has only f = 0. This converts the producer into a uniqueness statement, the corpus's strong suit.
- Kill: Krein's 1955 criterion in this form is classical, and the corpus has the first-crossing machinery: `XiShiftedHankelBoundaryKreinCrossing` (a first crossing has nonpositive Krein type, "excluding first crossings is a strict positive-type theorem"), `CCMRestrictedWeilPolarGroundState.polar_positivity_or_null_vector` (degeneracy law at apertures). Fourier form of the null equation: Σ_ρ f̂(γ_ρ)e^{−iγ_ρx} = 0 on (−L, L) with f̂ ∈ PW_L; the zero set is super-Nyquist, so such exponential relations exist in abundance and only the self-consistency c_ρ = f̂(γ_ρ) excludes them — which is positivity again. REDISCOVERED.
- Note for later: the window–height–depth dictionary (detection of depth δ at height γ at window ≍ log log γ/δ, i.e. Dirichlet polynomials of length (log γ)^{1/δ}) is corpus (`hcf-height-horizon`, row 537 correction). Do not re-derive.

## Row 546 — C546: growth bound of the scaling group on the Meyer cokernel (Sz.-Nagy / Gearhart–Prüss). Verdict: FALSE as stated (too strong), CIRCULAR when weakened
- Conjecture: the scaling group U_λ on the cokernel of E: g ↦ Σ_n g(n·) in an x^{±δ}-weighted L² space is uniformly bounded; by Sz.-Nagy it is then similar to a unitary group and every zero with |β − ½| < δ is critical.
- Kill: uniform boundedness forces the zero exponentials to form a Riesz system, which fails even under RH (close zero pairs give unbounded condition numbers; 1/ζ(σ+it) is not uniformly bounded for σ > ½). The weakening "growth bound zero" is, by Gearhart–Prüss, uniform resolvent bounds = bounds for 1/ζ in strips, i.e. RH-strength (Lindelöf for 1/ζ). In unweighted L² the group is unitary and only critical zeros are seen (Connes' absorption picture): off-line zeros are invisible there, not excluded.

## Row 547 — C547: Wall 13 test — odd positivity is copositivity on the pointwise cone; KKT and maximum principle at the minimizer. Verdict: FALSE (no leverage); law L547 [P]
- Wall named: 13 (cone mismatch: maximum principles act on pointwise cones, PBAP on the Bochner cone). Input: row 544 — in the odd sector the pointwise cone {g ≥ 0} parametrizes every odd test (F = g − g̃), so odd M-positivity is copositivity of N(x,y) = K_M(x−y) − K_M(x+y), a pointwise-cone statement.
- KKT at a negative minimizer g* ≥ 0, ∫g*² = 1, value λ < 0: the minimal representative has disjoint mirror supports (g*·g̃* = 0), and the multiplier conditions collapse to the odd eigen-equation K_M ⋆ F* = λF* on all of ℝ (on {g* > 0} directly, on its mirror by oddness, and off both the multiplier must vanish by oddness). So the copositive program has no slack: its minimizer is the odd ground state, typically sign-changing on the half-line.
- Maximum principle at the maximum x₀ of F*: every Markov (Lévy and ratio-atom) increment F*(x₀) − F*(y) is ≥ 0, including jumps across the mirror (min F* = −max F* by oddness); the conclusion needs the renormalized killing κ, which in the Doob frame is exactly 1 (row 539), giving only the trivial bound. Barta with a crossing-rate lower bound works only for sign-definite half profiles (row 533 territory), where the cusp crossing rate is about ½.
- L547 [P]: in the odd sector Bochner and pointwise positivity coincide (copositive = PSD for N), so Wall 13 is not the obstruction there; the obstruction is that the minimizer of the pointwise program is the sign-changing odd eigenfunction, on which no maximum principle has a sign. A producer must control sign-changing ground states, i.e. nodal structure (Courant/Sturm-type information) of the odd Weil eigenfunctions.
- Next conjecture direction (row 548): nodal/Sturm information. If the k-th odd eigenfunction of M on the window [−L, L] had at most k sign changes on (0, L) (a Sturm–oscillation law), then a negative odd eigenvalue would need a nodeless ground state on (0, L), i.e. a sign-definite half profile, which row 533's pole shielding controls. Test whether Sturm oscillation can hold for jump operators with arithmetic atoms.

## Row 548 — C548: Sturm oscillation / nodeless odd ground state; diamagnetic decomposition. Verdict: FALSE (no Perron–Frobenius), REDISCOVERED (Beurling–Deny); Phase 6/7 posted
- C548 (Sturm law): the lowest odd eigenfunction of M on every window has a sign-definite half profile. Then a first odd crossing would carry a nonnegative half profile f* with M(f* − f̃*) = 0, which pole shielding (row 533) excludes when off-line zeros are scarce. Kill: nodelessness of a ground state needs a positivity-improving semigroup; the odd half-line kernel J(x−y) − J(x+y) has negative singular parts on the product lines x + y = log n (δ-atoms beat any density there), so Perron–Frobenius fails structurally and Courant/Sturm nodal bounds are unavailable for nonlocal signed kernels. FALSE as a structural law.
- Diamagnetic identity (exact, odd F): M(F) = M(|F|) + 4Fr(F), Fr(F) = ∫∫_{x,y>0} [J(x−y)(F(x)F(y))₋ + J(x+y)(F(x)F(y))₊] ≥ 0, J = −K_M off the diagonal. This is the Beurling–Deny inequality M(|F|) ≤ M(F) of the Markov form M with its exact remainder; M(|F|) is an even nonnegative test and is typically very negative (Φ direction), so RH ⟺ 4Fr(F) ≥ −M(|F|) for all odd F. Signed-graph Cheeger bounds on Fr are quadratic and cannot reach the exact threshold (row 539 criticality). REDISCOVERED.
- Side computation (refines row 533, [P]): for a nonnegative half profile resonating with one off-line quadruple (f = b(1 + cos γ₀x)), the quadruple contributes at least −4(∫b sinh δx)² ≥ −16δ²P against the pole 8P (P = (∫b sinh(x/2))²), so one quadruple is invisible to nonnegative half profiles at every depth δ < ½; near the origin off-line zeros contribute positively (Re(δ + iγ)² < 0 for γ > δ). Nonnegative profiles see only collective, deep configurations.

## Row 549 — C549: Levinson's theorem for the cusp of the Riemann–Doob operator (count bound states by a scattering phase). Verdict: FALSE as a proof route; structural law L549 [P]
- Wall named: 12 (a producer must be a spectral realization). Input: the Doob operator E^Φ on L²(m), m = Φcosh(x/2)dx, is an unconditional self-adjoint operator whose sub-threshold odd eigenvalues are exactly the off-line zeros (row 539). Conjecture: its cusp carries a scattering theory, and Levinson's theorem (an index theorem: number of bound states = change of scattering phase / π) counts the off-line zeros through a computable phase.
- L549 [P] (spectrum): far-out functions see the killing rate (J⋆Φ)(x)/cosh(x/2) → 1 (exact criticality) plus a local jump part with degenerate coefficient a(x) = Φ(x)/cosh(x/2) → 0 super-exponentially acting through a log-order (Lévy) operator; choosing the oscillation frequency ≍ exp((λ−1)/a(x)) far out produces Weyl sequences for every λ ≥ 1, so σ_ess(E^Φ) = [1, ∞). At the threshold λ = 1 the generalized eigenfunctions are v = (Φ ⋆ h)/Φ with ĥ(±i/2) = 0 (the M-radical, K_M ⋆ (Φ⋆h) = 0), which grow like Φ(x − c)/Φ(x) ~ exp(πe^{2x}(1 − e^{−2c})): an infinite-dimensional space of non-normalizable threshold resonances.
- Kill: Levinson's theorem needs a reference dynamics at the cusp (wave operators) and a finite threshold structure; here the cusp is degenerate (coefficient → 0 faster than any exponential) and the threshold resonance space is infinite-dimensional, so the operator sits at maximal criticality: any perturbation can spawn bound states and no finite phase count exists. This is the spectral face of exact criticality (rows 532e, 539) and of the near-radical family.
- Also checked and dropped: the Φ′ ground-state frame M(Φ′u) = ∫∫_{x,y>0}(u(x)−u(y))²|Φ′(x)Φ′(y)|[J(x−y) − J(x+y)] + ∫_0^∞u²|Φ′|sinh(x/2) (u even; exact, from K_M ⋆ Φ′ = −½sinh(x/2)) is row 532e (cusp balance ¼ − 2·⅛ = 0); and the window flow dλ₁/dL ≥ −cλ₁ is Krein first-crossing (row 545).

## Row 550 — C550: radical-frame duality and the CCM frontier read from Lean. Verdict: REDISCOVERED; law L550 [P] (universal frame criticality)
- Corpus frontier (read in Lean, not docs): `CCMRestrictedWeilFullFormRealRootedGround.exists_realRooted_fullGround` — real-rooted ground states of the restricted Weil form exist at every aperture, by the division trick (a non-real transform zero w₀ splits f̂ = (w − w₀)ĝ into (w − Re w₀)ĝ and (Im w₀)ĝ; the pair identity (w − w₀)(w − w̄₀) = (w − Re w₀)² + (Im w₀)² splits both |f̂|² on ℝ and the pole term at the conjugate polar points, so both pieces are ground states). Capstones `CCMEvenFullGroundSecondMomentRH`, `CCMEvenFullGroundZeroSumRH`, `CCMFullGroundGaussianGrowthRH`: RH follows from a uniform bound |∫t²f_j| ≤ S|∫f_j| (equivalently Σ 1/x_k² ≤ S for the real zeros) on even real-rooted ground states. The open core is localization of ground states: under RH the ground state is the Φ-like truncation (energy ≍ exp(−2πe^{2a})), under ¬RH it jumps to a γ₀-oscillating wide test; localization is positivity in disguise.
- The division trick applies verbatim to M and to the odd sector of my frame (the odd pole term −2F₊F₋ = 2S² sits at the conjugate pair ±i/2), so odd M ground states are real-rooted too; real-rootedness does not prevent negativity (an odd real-rooted H can place a real zero under an off-line γ₀, making Re H(γ₀ − iδ)² < 0).
- L550 [P] (frame duality): for any radical Ψ (Ψ̂ = Ξh), K_M ⋆ Ψ = −2[cosh(x/2)∫Ψcosh(y/2) − sinh(x/2)∫Ψsinh(y/2)] exactly, so the ground-state representation M(Ψu) = ½∫∫(u(x)−u(y))²Ψ(x)Ψ(y)J(x−y) + ∫u²Ψ(K_M⋆Ψ) gives two dual frames for odd F: even Ψ > 0 (kinetic term fully Markov, no frustration; potential −2C_ΨΨcosh(x/2) < 0, so a gap is needed) and odd Ψ positive on (0,∞) (potential 2S_ΨΨsinh(x/2) ≥ 0; kinetic term frustrated across the mirror by the product atoms). In both, the jump rate into the core at the cusp equals the potential at PNT main order (the explicit formula), so every radical frame is exactly critical at the cusp; the next order is the zero side. Mixed (pair-dependent) frames lose the exact convolution identity. No frame escapes Wall 1.

## Rows 551–556 — quick kill batch (each names the wall and the input). Verdicts: FALSE / REDISCOVERED
- C551 (arithmetic Kähler identity): an sl₂-triple (L, Λ, H) compatible with W would give Hodge–Riemann positivity from primitive decompositions (oscillator sl₂: L = x², Λ = −d²/dx², Hermite weight vectors). Kill: W is translation-invariant, so a compatible Lefschetz operator must satisfy W(Lf, g) = W(f, Lg) for all tests and commute with the translation-invariant structure; only Fourier multipliers do, and those generate an abelian algebra (no sl₂). The metaplectic sl₂ moves the zero-side evaluation points. FALSE (Wall 5).
- C552 (Schur products across places): each local factor Γ_ℝ(½+it), (1 − p^{−½−it})^{−1} is positive-definite in t (Fourier transform of a positive measure), so finite Euler products times Γ_ℝ are positive-definite (Schur/Bochner); Ξ itself is positive-definite (Φ > 0, Pólya). Positive-definiteness bounds |Ξ(t)| ≤ Ξ(0) but says nothing about where zeros lie. REDISCOVERED (Wall 15, support vs positivity).
- C553 (Herglotz positivity of local log-derivatives): Re[(1 + z_p)/(1 − z_p)] > 0 for z_p = p^{−s}, Re s > 0, so each local factor satisfies Re(−L_p′/L_p) + (log p)/2 > 0 on the whole half-plane Re s > 0 (Poisson kernel at radius p^{−σ}); the global sum needs Σ_p (log p)/2 = ∞ renormalized by the pole, and the renormalized sum converges in the strip only under quasi-RH. REDISCOVERED (Wall 6; Harnack gives only the trivial bound 2log p/(√p − 1) per prime).
- C554 (GUE rigidity): bulk statistics cannot see a density-zero off-line set. Wall 7. FALSE as a route.
- C555 (variational characterization of ζ among functional-equation solutions, Weil form as Hessian): variations of FE-solutions live on coefficient/frequency space, the Weil form on test functions; the explicit formula pairing them is linear in the zero measure, so its second variation vanishes; Hamburger rigidity makes the integer-frequency solution space one-dimensional anyway. FALSE.
- C556 (horocycle/mixing rates): RH ⟺ closed-horocycle equidistribution at rate y^{3/4−ε} (Zagier); mixing rates come from the spectral gap of SL₂(ℤ)\ℍ (λ₁ ≈ 91.1), the Eisenstein part carries ζ(2s) only through its scattering poles. REDISCOVERED (free-family law, row 364).
- Learning: six distinct mechanism classes die at walls 5, 6, 7, 15 and the free-family law within one step each; the remaining candidates all have to produce an exact spectral realization with multiplicative structure (walls 11–12), which is the corpus's Door 1.
